# SpecKiwi Node.js Implementation SRS

| Field | Value |

| Document Type | scope_srs |

| Last Updated | 2026-05-13 |
| Rules | [SRS-MD Authoring Rules v2.5.0](../rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v2.5.0.md) |

## 1. Scope Overview

이 문서는 SpecKiwi v1.0.0의 Node.js LTS 기반 TypeScript ESM 구현 구조, core module, patch engine, testing, package scripts를 정의한다.

## 2. Scope Boundaries

### In Scope

- Node.js LTS runtime
- TypeScript ESM package
- core module layout
- line-based patch engine
- MCP SDK adapter
- CLI adapter
- test fixtures and scripts

### Out of Scope

- 브라우저 런타임
- CommonJS dual package
- Rust/Python 구현
- 웹 서버 제품화
- 데이터베이스 저장소

## 3. Assumptions and Constraints

- Node.js 24 LTS를 권장한다.
- package metadata는 `package.json`에 둔다.
- MCP SDK 의존성은 adapter에 격리한다.

## 4. Requirements

### CON-NODE-001 — Node.js LTS 기반 구현

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | constraint |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | nodejs, runtime, lts |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi v1.0.0은 Node.js LTS 런타임을 기준으로 구현해야 한다.

#### Rationale

저장소 로컬 CLI와 MCP server는 JavaScript/TypeScript 생태계에서 쉽게 실행되어야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: package `engines.node`는 `>=22`를 선언한다.
- [x] AC-2: 문서에서는 Node.js 24 LTS를 권장한다.
- [x] AC-3: EOL Node.js 버전에 대한 지원을 요구하지 않는다.

- [x] AC-3: `src/core/query`는 filter와 summary를 담당한다.
- [x] AC-4: `src/core/mutation`은 line-based patch를 담당한다.
- [x] AC-5: `src/core/format`은 CLI 출력 formatter를 담당한다.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi는 Markdown 변경 명령을 line-based patch engine으로 구현해야 한다.

#### Rationale

Markdown AST 재출력은 불필요한 diff를 만들 가능성이 높다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Status row 변경은 해당 line만 교체한다.
- [x] AC-2: AC 체크 변경은 해당 task line만 교체한다.
- [x] AC-3: Evidence 추가는 table 끝에 한 row를 삽입한다.
- [x] AC-4: Trace 추가는 table 끝에 한 row를 삽입한다.

SpecKiwi는 Windows, macOS, Linux에서 path 처리와 줄바꿈 처리를 안정적으로 수행해야 한다.

#### Rationale

Node.js CLI는 다양한 개발 환경에서 실행된다.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core와 MCP server는 사용자가 제공한 입력을 shell command로 실행하지 않아야 한다.

#### Rationale

SRS 도구가 agent에 연결되므로 임의 명령 실행은 보안 위험이다.

### FR-NODE-008 — Requirement Block 생성 mutation service

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | core, mutation, requirement, markdown |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core는 CLI와 MCP가 공유하는 Requirement Block 생성 mutation service를 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

새 요구사항 추가는 Markdown 원본을 직접 수정하는 고위험 mutation이므로 CLI와 MCP adapter가 각자 구현하지 않고 같은 core 계약과 검증을 사용해야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: service input은 type, scope, target, title, requirement, acceptance criteria records를 필수로 받는다.
- [x] AC-2: service input은 status, priority, tags, risk, stability, verification method, rationale, optional sections, evidence rows, trace rows를 선택적으로 받는다.
- [x] AC-3: service는 `00.index.md`의 Scope Map과 Target Map으로 scope와 target 존재 여부를 검증한다.
- [x] AC-4: service는 type 값을 ID prefix로 매핑하고 저장소 전체 기존 Requirement ID를 스캔하여 다음 번호를 자동 생성한다.
- [x] AC-5: service는 discarded 상태의 ID도 재사용하지 않는다.
- [x] AC-6: service는 SRS-MD 필수 metadata table과 고정 section을 포함한 Requirement Block을 생성한다.
- [x] AC-7: service는 새 block을 대상 scope 문서의 `## 4. Requirements` section 끝에 line-based patch로 삽입하고 기존 newline style을 보존한다.
- [x] AC-8: service는 `verified` 상태 생성 시 모든 AC checked, Verification Evidence 1개 이상, evidence reference non-empty 조건을 적용한다.
- [x] AC-9: dry run은 파일을 변경하지 않고 생성될 ID, 대상 파일, patch summary, diagnostics를 반환한다.

### FR-NODE-009 — 프로젝트 초기화 bootstrap service

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | core, init, bootstrap, rules, agent |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core는 CLI와 MCP가 공유하는 프로젝트 초기화 bootstrap service를 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

SRS 구조 생성은 파일 시스템을 변경하는 mutation이므로 CLI와 MCP adapter가 각자 구현하지 않고 같은 템플릿, 경로 안전성, 중복 삽입 방지 정책을 사용해야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: service input은 root를 필수로 받고 target, scope, force를 선택적으로 받는다.
- [x] AC-2: service는 root 내부의 `docs/`, `docs/spec/`, `docs/rule/` 구조를 확인하고 없으면 생성한다.
- [x] AC-3: `docs/spec/00.index.md`가 없으면 요구사항이 없는 기본 SRS index 문서를 생성한다.
- [x] AC-4: `docs/spec/90.appendix.md`가 없으면 기본 appendix 문서를 생성한다.
- [x] AC-5: `docs/rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md`가 없으면 canonical SRS-MD rules 문서를 생성한다.
- [x] AC-6: service는 대문자 `AGENTS.md`와 `CLAUDE.md`를 항상 생성 또는 갱신한다.
- [x] AC-7: agent 안내 managed block은 `# SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 v1.3` heading, `<!-- /SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 -->` suffix marker, SRS source-of-truth 강제 문장, TDD 원칙, current work status workflow를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-8: service는 동일한 현재 버전 managed block이 이미 있으면 중복 삽입하지 않고, 다른 버전 또는 legacy unversioned block이 있으면 현재 버전 block으로 교체한다.
- [x] AC-9: service는 `force`가 없으면 기존 파일을 덮어쓰지 않고 skipped path로 보고한다.
- [x] AC-10: service는 root 밖 경로 쓰기를 거부하고 shell command를 실행하지 않는다.
- [x] AC-11: service result는 created, skipped, updated path 목록과 diagnostics를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-12: MCP server startup은 이 service를 사용해 `docs/spec/00.index.md`가 없는 root를 자동 초기화한다.
- [x] AC-13: index template은 metadata table에 빈 `Active Target` row를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-14: `init --target`은 Target Map에 planned row를 만들 수 있지만 `Active Target` 값은 비워 둔다.
- [x] AC-15: core parser는 `Active Target` metadata를 `index.activeTarget`으로 노출하고, row가 누락된 구형 index에서만 Target Map의 `active` row로 fallback한다.
- [x] AC-16: core mutation service는 등록된 target만 active target으로 설정하고 `Active Target` row와 Target Map status를 함께 갱신한다.

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- index template은 요구사항이 없는 상태에서도 SRS-MD index 필수 heading과 Target Map, Scope Map table을 포함해야 한다.
- index template은 agent가 현재 작업 목표 버전을 빠르게 찾도록 빈 `Active Target` metadata row를 포함해야 한다.
- target 입력은 Target Map row 생성에만 사용하며 active target 선택으로 간주하지 않는다.
- `scope`가 제공되면 기본 scope SRS 생성은 별도 구현 결정이 필요하지만, v1.0.0 최소 동작은 빈 index와 rules 생성이다.
- agent 파일 선택 입력은 core service 계약에 포함하지 않는다. CLI/MCP init은 항상 `AGENTS.md`와 `CLAUDE.md` 모두를 upsert한다.
- MCP adapter는 server startup에서 root를 결정한 뒤 index 누락 여부를 확인하고, 누락 시 이 service를 호출한다.
- rules 문서의 canonical 위치는 `docs/rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md`다.
- agent instruction의 현재 managed block version은 `v1.3`이다.
- service는 managed heading과 suffix marker로 block 범위를 산정하여 오래된 block 전체를 교체한다.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core는 `00.index.md`의 Completed Work Log를 파싱, 조회, 검증, 추가하는 공유 service를 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

CLI와 MCP adapter가 같은 core service를 사용해야 Completed Work Log의 filter, dry-run, 입력 거부, newline 보존 동작이 일관된다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: core type은 `CompletedWorkEntry`와 `IndexDocument.completedWork`를 제공한다.
- [x] AC-2: parser는 Completed Work Log가 없거나 비어 있어도 `completedWork: []`를 반환한다.
- [x] AC-3: query service는 `target`, `scope`, `since`, `limit` filter를 지원한다.
- [x] AC-4: target filter는 Target cell이 비어 있는 cross-target row를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-5: validator는 Completed Work Log inconsistency를 `SRS-W011`부터 `SRS-W015`까지 warning으로 보고한다.
- [x] AC-6: mutation service는 기존 table append, section-only table repair, missing section creation을 지원한다.
- [x] AC-7: mutation service는 invalid date와 pipe-containing cell input을 파일 변경 전에 거부한다.
- [x] AC-8: mutation service는 dry-run patch summary를 반환하고 기존 newline style을 보존한다.

### FR-NODE-011 — Shared Markdown table cell safety for mutations

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.2.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mutation, table, safety |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/06.hardening-governance-research.md](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core mutation services must use one shared Markdown table-cell safety policy for every mutation path that writes pipe table cells.

#### Rationale

The parser uses constrained GFM tables, so unescaped pipe or newline characters in mutation input can corrupt metadata, evidence, trace, or completed-work rows.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A shared helper rejects pipe `|` and newline characters in table cell inputs for all table-writing mutations.
- [x] AC-2: `add-requirement` rejects unsafe metadata, evidence, trace, research, implementation note, and change note table cells before writing.
- [x] AC-3: `add-evidence` and `add-trace` reject unsafe table cells before writing.
- [x] AC-4: `add-completed-work` uses the same helper and no separate policy fork.
- [x] AC-5: Mutation failures return structured errors and leave target files unchanged.

#### Verification Evidence

| VE-1 | command | npm run build; npm run typecheck; npm run lint; npm test; npm run test:integration; npm run release:acceptance; npm run perf:srs | all | P9 bash verification passed on 2026-05-10 |
| VE-2 | command | npm run release:check | all | P9 release readiness passed on 2026-05-10 |

| Requirement | FR-MCP-012 | related_to | MCP completed-work mutation exposes the policy. |

### FR-NODE-012 — Package-relative bundled rules loading

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |

| Target | v1.2.0 |

| Priority | critical |
| Tags | init, package, rules |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi init must load the bundled rules document from the installed package artifact rather than from the caller current working directory.

#### Rationale

Installed CLI usage can run outside the source repository; cwd-relative rules loading can create fallback stub rules instead of the full bundled rules document.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `loadBundledRulesDocument` or equivalent runtime logic resolves the rules template relative to the package artifact.
- [x] AC-2: `npm pack` or equivalent package smoke test proves the full `docs/rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md` is included and readable after install.
- [x] AC-3: Running `speckiwi init` from an external project creates the full rules document, not a fallback stub, when the package artifact contains the rules file.
- [x] AC-4: Fallback stub behavior is reserved for genuine missing artifact failures and reports a diagnostic or warning.

### FR-NODE-013 — Optimistic concurrency for generated-ID mutations

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.2.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mutation, concurrency, generated-id |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/06.hardening-governance-research.md](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi mutation services that generate IDs or insert rows must detect stale files before writing and avoid silent ID collisions or misplaced inserts.

#### Rationale

Concurrent CLI/MCP mutations can choose the same next Requirement ID or write against an outdated section boundary unless the patch layer verifies file freshness.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: File read metadata includes a hash, mtime/size, or equivalent snapshot token used before write.
- [x] AC-2: Before write mode applies a patch, the service rechecks that the target file still matches the snapshot or returns a stale patch error.
- [x] AC-3: Generated Requirement IDs are revalidated against the latest parsed records before insertion.
- [x] AC-4: Insert operations validate their neighboring lines or section-end preconditions before applying the patch.
- [x] AC-5: On a safe generated-ID conflict, the service either retries once after reparse or returns explicit retry guidance.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | command | npm run build; npm run typecheck; npm run lint; npm test; npm run test:integration; npm run release:acceptance; npm run perf:srs | all | P9 bash verification passed on 2026-05-10 |
| VE-2 | command | npm run release:check | all | P9 release readiness passed on 2026-05-10 |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-002 | depends_on | Patch engine must support stale-file protection. |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-008 | depends_on | Requirement ID generation needs collision protection. |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-010 | related_to | Completed-work row insertion can use the same stale insert guard. |

#### Research / Analysis

- [SpecKiwi Hardening and Governance Research](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md) sections 6.6 and 7.2.

#### Implementation Notes

- Start with optimistic checks; lock files are out of scope unless optimistic concurrency proves insufficient.

- [x] AC-3: A `test:coverage` script reports coverage for parser, validator, mutation, workflow, CLI, and MCP layers.

- [x] AC-5: Release readiness for v1.2.0 cannot be considered complete until CI gates pass.

### FR-NODE-014 — target lifecycle mutation and default target resolution

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.1.0 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | core, mutation, target, active-target |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/spec/30.cli-interface.srs.md](./30.cli-interface.srs.md), [docs/spec/40.mcp-stdio-interface.srs.md](./40.mcp-stdio-interface.srs.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core must provide shared services for safe Target Map row creation, Active Target assignment, and Active Target default resolution for target-scoped mutations.

#### Rationale

Target lifecycle changes affect index consistency and every newly authored Requirement Block. Keeping this logic in core prevents CLI and MCP from diverging and avoids raw Markdown edits by agents.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Core target mutation can register a missing target only when creation is explicitly requested.
- [x] AC-2: Registered target rows include target, type, status, and description fields rendered in the Target Map table.
- [x] AC-3: Setting a target active updates the `Active Target` metadata row and ensures no other Target Map row remains `active`.
- [x] AC-4: Existing behavior is preserved when creation is not requested: unknown targets are rejected without file changes.
- [x] AC-5: Requirement creation accepts an optional target and resolves a missing target from `workspace.index.activeTarget`.
- [x] AC-6: Missing explicit target plus empty Active Target returns a structured mutation error without file changes.
- [x] AC-7: Requirement creation output records the resolved target and target source.
- [x] AC-8: Tests cover CRLF/newline preservation, dry-run behavior, unknown target rejection, active row replacement, and CLI/MCP parity through shared services.

#### Research / Analysis

- The default target source enum should distinguish `explicit` and `active-target`.
- Target row insertion should use the existing line-based patch engine and stale snapshot guard.
- Defaults should be deterministic, for example type `version` and a non-empty generated description when the user omits a description.

### FR-NODE-015 — Completed Work Log report path core support

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.1.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | core, completed-work, mutation, report-paths |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/spec/20.parser-validation.srs.md](./20.parser-validation.srs.md), [docs/rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v2.5.0.md](../rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v2.5.0.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core must allow Completed Work Log mutations to persist an optional list of completion report paths and expose that list through shared query results.

#### Rationale

CLI and MCP must share one implementation for report path persistence so Completed Work Log behavior remains consistent across interfaces.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: AddCompletedWorkInput accepts an optional reportPaths string array; absent, null-equivalent, or empty arrays are treated as no report paths.
- [x] AC-2: Mutation validates reportPaths against the path grammar defined by FR-PARSE-016 and rejects empty-after-trim, absolute, parent-traversal, URL, backslash, pipe, comma, CR/LF, and fragment-marker values.
- [x] AC-3: When reportPaths are absent or empty and the existing Completed Work Log table has five columns, mutation preserves the legacy five-column table.
- [x] AC-4: When the existing Completed Work Log table already has Report Paths, mutation writes a blank trailing Report Paths cell for rows with no report paths.
- [x] AC-5: When reportPaths are provided for a legacy five-column table, mutation atomically upgrades the header, separator, and existing rows to six columns before appending the new row.
- [x] AC-6: Mutation serializes reportPaths as comma-separated path tokens in a trailing Report Paths cell, preserving caller order and duplicates.
- [x] AC-7: Mutation and query JSON outputs include reportPaths as a string array while preserving existing date, target, scope, requirementIds, summary, written, and patch fields.
- [x] AC-8: Dry-run mutation output shows the same reportPaths and table-upgrade patch that a write would perform without modifying files.
- [x] AC-9: Human-oriented output suppresses empty reportPaths fields while still showing non-empty report path lists.
- [x] AC-10: Generated index templates include the Report Paths column in the Completed Work Log table.
- [x] AC-11: Bootstrap templates, SRS-MD rules, appendix schemas, and README command examples are updated so generated projects and documentation share the same Completed Work Log contract.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/add-completed-work.test.ts; test/core/mutation/table-cell-safety.test.ts; test/core/query/completed-work.test.ts; test/core/bootstrap/init-project.test.ts | all | - |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-010 | depends_on | Extends the existing Completed Work Log core service. |
| Requirement | FR-PARSE-016 | depends_on | Uses the parsed report path list in the index document. |
| Requirement | IR-CLI-020 | supports | CLI add-completed-work delegates to this core mutation. |
| Requirement | FR-MCP-012 | supports | MCP add_completed_work delegates to this core mutation. |

#### Research / Analysis

- 2026-05-13 feasibility review concluded this is a core contract migration, not only a CLI/MCP flag.
- The main compatibility risk is malformed row rendering when fixed five-cell mutation logic writes into a six-column table.
- The recommended compatibility rule is to preserve legacy five-column logs unless report paths are provided or the table already has Report Paths.

#### Implementation Notes

- Use a trailing Report Paths column serialized as comma-separated repository-relative paths. Keep Completed Work Log as summary metadata and do not treat report paths as Verification Evidence.
- Implement header-aware row rendering before wiring CLI or MCP options.

### FR-NODE-016 — Kiwi skill installation core service

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.2.3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | skills, install, core, utf-8, local-llm |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core must provide a reusable skill installation service that copies validated repository-managed Kiwi skill trees from canonical source roots into agent-specific project or global skill directories while preserving UTF-8 content, relative resources, safe paths, dry-run diagnostics, and conflict controls.

#### Rationale

The CLI and future MCP or automation surfaces should not each implement their own skill-copy logic. A shared core service keeps install paths, safety checks, dry-run behavior, and validation consistent while allowing the repository to maintain separate canonical source trees for Codex, Claude, and OpenCode/Hermes local-LLM variants.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The service accepts an explicit target agent value at minimum for Codex, Claude, OpenCode, and Hermes, and resolves the canonical source root for each supported target.
- [x] AC-2: The service treats `skills/etc` as the canonical source root for OpenCode and Hermes local-LLM Kiwi variants and must not create or require a `skills/llm` source root.
- [x] AC-3: The service supports project-local installation, global installation, and a safe custom destination root, with global paths resolved per target agent, Hermes global installs scoped by category, and no reliance on locale-dependent path handling.
- [x] AC-4: The service validates each selected skill directory before copying, including required `SKILL.md` or an explicitly supported legacy source entrypoint normalization to installed `SKILL.md`, frontmatter name matching the directory, UTF-8 readability, and referenced `scripts`, `references`, or `assets` that must remain reachable after install.
- [x] AC-5: The service reports a deterministic dry-run plan that includes source root, destination root, operation (`install`, `update`, `skip`, or `conflict`), copied files, skipped files, removed stale files, conflicts, and an MCP preflight status of `satisfied`, `missing`, or `not_checked`.
- [x] AC-6: The service treats an existing valid same-identity destination skill as an `update`, treats a byte-identical destination as `skip`, refuses unsafe or non-skill destination conflicts, and conflict results must not partially copy a skill.
- [x] AC-7: The service rejects path traversal, absolute-path injection inside skill names, unsupported target names, and non-directory skill sources before any file mutation.
- [x] AC-8: The service preserves executable helper scripts and relative resource layout without rewriting skill content unless a separate migration or documented install-time entrypoint normalization step explicitly owns that transformation.
- [x] AC-9: The distributed package includes the canonical bundled skill source roots required by the installer (`skills/codex`, `skills/claude`, and `skills/etc`) or returns a structured source-unavailable error before planning any destination mutation.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | command | npx vitest run test/core/skills/install-skill.test.ts test/cli/skills-cli.test.ts test/smoke/package.test.ts --hookTimeout 60000 --testTimeout 120000 | FR-NODE-016 AC-1 AC-2 AC-3 AC-4 AC-5 AC-6 AC-7 AC-8 AC-9 | - |
| VE-2 | command | npm run typecheck; npm run lint; npm run build; npm run test | FR-NODE-016 AC-1 AC-3 AC-4 AC-6 AC-7 AC-8 | - |
| VE-3 | subagent-review | Locke subagent review 019e40a3-c101-7b12-a10e-3739af9385e7 on 2026-05-20 | FR-NODE-016 AC-1 AC-2 AC-3 AC-4 AC-5 AC-6 AC-7 AC-8 AC-9 | - |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Requirement | FR-FLOW-012 | depends_on | The installer must preserve the mandatory MCP normal-operation rule in installed Kiwi skills. |

#### Research / Analysis

- OpenCode discovers `.opencode/skills`, `~/.config/opencode/skills`, `.claude/skills`, and `.agents/skills`. Hermes uses `~/.hermes/skills` as its primary read-write location and can scan external skill directories. These discovery models support a core installer that separates canonical repository source trees from agent-specific runtime destinations.

#### Implementation Notes

- The service should live under core rather than the CLI adapter. Target-path mapping should be table-driven and covered by Windows and POSIX-style tests. The `skills/etc` source root is a repository distribution source, not necessarily a direct runtime discovery path for every agent.
- [2026-05-19] 2026-05-20 research: user clarified that installing a skill whose destination already exists is an update operation, not an overwrite conflict. AC-6 should be revised before implementation: valid same-identity existing skill => operation=update; only unsafe/non-skill destination conflicts should fail unless an explicit recovery option is provided.
- [2026-05-20] Phase 0 contract repair: same-identity means matching destination folder name, source/destination frontmatter `name`, and target-specific identity fields; for Hermes, `category` is part of identity. Symlinks in source or destination skill trees are rejected for the MVP.

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-05-19 | Created | add-requirement |

### FR-NODE-017 — Unified mutation envelope

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, tools, mutation, envelope, CAND-NODE-MUTATION-ENVELOPE |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core mutation services must return one shared mutation envelope for SRS-changing operations so CLI and MCP adapters can expose dry-run state, write state, stale guards, patch operations, previews, updated records, diagnostics, and structured errors consistently.

#### Rationale

The v2.3.0 research found that inconsistent mutation results hide unsafe writes, drop context, and make agents fall back to raw Markdown edits. A shared core envelope is the prerequisite for safe adapter parity and workflow mutation tooling.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Core mutation success results include mutation.kind, mutation.filePath, mutation.dryRun, mutation.written, mutation.operations, mutation.preview, updated record data when applicable, diagnostics, and diagnosticsSummary.
- [x] AC-2: Core mutation failure results include a structured error code, message, diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, and any safe stale-guard context needed for recovery.
- [x] AC-3: Dry-run results set mutation.written to false and expose the planned operations without changing files.
- [x] AC-4: Mutation operations include enough line or hunk detail for CLI and MCP adapters to display a minimal patch preview without reparsing raw Markdown.
- [x] AC-5: Existing mutation services for requirement creation, status changes, AC checks, evidence additions, trace additions, stability updates, target goal updates, target activation, and completed-work additions are covered by parity tests for the shared envelope.

### FR-NODE-018 — Index summary synchronization for mutations

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, index, summary, mutation, CAND-NODE-INDEX-SUMMARY-SYNC |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must keep Status Summary and Requirement Type Summary consistent after tool-owned requirement, status, and type-affecting mutations, either by updating the index rollups during the mutation or by providing an explicit sync-index operation with dry-run preview and CLI/MCP access.

#### Rationale

The v2.3.0 research found that add_requirement can leave the workspace with Status Summary and Requirement Type Summary drift warnings. A safe mutation path that creates validation warnings makes agents distrust the tool and patch index Markdown manually.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Requirement creation through core services does not leave Status Summary or Requirement Type Summary drift after the tool flow completes, unless the caller explicitly requested dry-run.
- [x] AC-2: Status-changing mutations update or preview the affected Status Summary counts when the workspace has an index rollup table.
- [x] AC-3: Type-affecting requirement mutations update or preview Requirement Type Summary counts when the workspace has an index rollup table.
- [x] AC-4: If synchronization is exposed as a separate sync-index operation, the operation supports dry-run, stale guards, patch preview, and the shared mutation envelope from FR-NODE-017.
- [x] AC-5: CLI and MCP tests cover add-requirement, update-status, and sync-index scenarios and validate that validate --fail-on-warning remains clean after the supported tool flow.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide safe structured requirement editing services for title, statement, metadata, acceptance criteria, verification evidence rows, and trace link rows so agents can maintain Requirement Blocks without raw Markdown patches.

#### Rationale

The research found that normal maintenance still requires direct Markdown edits for many fields. Granular editing must use the same safety model as other mutations so agents can update SRS text, ACs, evidence, and traces with dry-run previews and stale guards.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Core services can update requirement title, requirement statement, priority, risk, tags, related docs, verification method, GitHub issue, and other supported metadata without rewriting unrelated sections.
- [x] AC-2: Core services can add, update, delete, and reorder acceptance criteria while preserving deterministic AC identifiers and preventing unsafe state transitions for verified requirements.
- [x] AC-3: Core services can update and delete Verification Evidence rows and Trace Links rows with table-cell safety validation before writing.
- [x] AC-4: All granular editing operations support dry-run, stale guards, Change Notes policy, the shared mutation envelope from FR-NODE-017, and updated record output.
- [x] AC-5: CLI and MCP adapter tests prove each granular operation is available through an official tool or command before skills rely on it.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must include realistic MCP-first and CLI-fallback coding-agent workflow fixtures that measure payload size, detect unsupported raw-edit fallbacks, and verify that official tools can complete common SRS discovery and mutation loops without manual Markdown editing.

#### Rationale

The research found that existing tests cover individual features but not the end-to-end agent trust path. A fixture corpus is needed to prove tools actually reduce abandonment in real workflows.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Fixtures cover MCP-first requirement discovery, MCP mutation preview, MCP mutation write, CLI fallback discovery, CLI fallback mutation, target selection, missing evidence checks, and completed-work lookup.
- [x] AC-2: Fixtures fail when a manual SRS-MD edit occurs without a captured command or tool error, affected requirement ID, active target, and follow-up SRS candidate label.
- [x] AC-3: Payload measurement fixtures record baselineBytes, baselineApproxTokens, compactBytes, compactApproxTokens, requiredFieldsPresent, and reductionRatio for compact official tool paths.
- [x] AC-4: Workflow tests include both successful tool paths and degraded paths where a documented tool gap allows a manual workaround with explicit evidence.
- [x] AC-5: The fixture corpus is tied to v2.3.0 requirements and can be run in CI without depending on an external MCP client process unless the test explicitly marks itself as MCP integration.

| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi release and smoke tooling must verify package version alignment, packed skill paths, MCP startup metadata, installed MCP availability, and package-version reporting before agents depend on installed CLI or MCP behavior.

#### Rationale

The research found package identity drift and MCP preflight weaknesses. If installed tools do not match repository expectations, agents cannot trust CLI or MCP behavior even when source tests pass.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Package smoke tests verify package.json, package-lock.json, built metadata, CLI version output, and MCP server metadata all report the same package version.
- [x] AC-2: Packed package tests verify bundled skill paths and entrypoint casing for Codex, Claude, OpenCode, Hermes, and local-LLM variants that the package claims to install.
- [x] AC-3: A doctor or smoke command checks MCP startup, root resolution, tool listing, active-target read, validation read, and at least one dry-run-safe mutation path against the installed package.
- [x] AC-4: Doctor output includes structured JSON diagnostics and recommended remediation commands when package metadata, MCP startup, or skill path checks fail.
- [x] AC-5: Release gates fail when package smoke expectations are stale relative to package metadata or when installed MCP metadata reports a hard-coded version.

### FR-NODE-020 — Workflow artifact resolver

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide a shared workflow artifact resolver that discovers current and legacy plan, sidecar, validator, analysis, pipeline, PM session, coder state, and worklog artifacts with safe path validation, deterministic scoring, ambiguity diagnostics, and companion-file resolution.

#### Rationale

The Kiwi planner research found that CLI, MCP, and skills currently use separate path rules for workflow artifacts. A shared resolver is required before compact read tools can safely choose a plan, session, or pipeline file.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The resolver supports explicit path, explicit run ID, current docs/plans artifacts, current .kiwi session artifacts, legacy docs/plan artifacts, legacy .snoworca session artifacts, and repository pipeline files.
- [x] AC-2: Candidate scoring applies documented signals for explicit match, current contract path, target match, plan and sidecar run ID match, generated_at, legacy penalty, target mismatch, and parse errors.
- [x] AC-3: Tie-breaking uses generated_at descending, file mtime descending, and relative path ascending; unresolved equal candidates return ambiguous_artifact unless the caller explicitly allows ambiguity.
- [x] AC-4: Companion discovery uses plan frontmatter sidecar_path, basename matching, same-directory search, and missing-companion diagnostics in that order.
- [x] AC-5: Resolver outputs include relative path, absolute-safe workspace root, artifact kind, legacy flag, confidence, hashes or mtimes needed for resume guards, diagnostics, and diagnosticsSummary.

### FR-NODE-021 — Workflow JSONL core utilities

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide shared JSONL workflow utilities for pipeline and worklog parsing, tailing, appending, locking, trailing-LF enforcement, schema-version validation, invalid-line diagnostics, duplicate event-key detection, best-effort versus halt append policy, and correction-chain latest-state calculation.

#### Rationale

The Kiwi planner research found that pipeline and worklog state is currently read by convention. Structural JSONL tools are needed to avoid hallucinated state, missed invalid lines, and unsafe append behavior.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The JSONL parser reads one JSON object per line and reports invalid lines with line number, byte offset when available, raw-safe excerpt, diagnostics, and exclusion from latest-state selection.
- [x] AC-2: Pipeline utilities compute eventKey from skill and run_id and detect duplicate event keys, missing correction targets, correction cycles, and correction chains deeper than the configured maximum.
- [x] AC-3: Correction handling excludes corrected events from latest-state selection while keeping corrected events visible in tail output with correctedBy metadata.
- [x] AC-4: Append utilities support single-line JSON serialization, trailing LF enforcement, schema-version validation, lock or stale guard semantics, configurable best-effort versus halt policy, and return the shared mutation envelope from FR-NODE-017 for write operations.
- [x] AC-5: Tests cover invalid JSONL lines, missing or unsupported schema versions, missing trailing LF, duplicate event keys, correction missing target, correction depth, correction cycles, chronological tailing, latest-state selection, best-effort versus halt append policy, and dry-run append behavior.

### REL-NODE-003 — Workflow artifact validators and hallucination guards

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must validate planner sidecars, PM state, coder state, pipeline events, worklog events, task dependency order, resume hashes, checklist display state, and legacy trace fields before tools recommend next tasks or resume actions.

#### Rationale

The Kiwi planner research found field drift and ambiguous execution state across plan, sidecar, PM, coder, pipeline, and worklog artifacts. Validators must fail closed instead of letting agents infer unsafe next actions.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Validators treat sidecar tasks as the task catalog source of truth, depends_on_task as the ordering source of truth, PM state as execution status source of truth, and worklog as append-only audit evidence.
- [x] AC-2: Validators detect dependency cycles, blocked dependencies, PM and plan checkbox drift, PM and coder state conflict, stale plan or sidecar hashes, stale locks, invalid JSONL lines, and invalid correction chains.
- [x] AC-3: Validators normalize task.req_ids as the canonical task-to-requirement field and report legacy task.traces req_id fields as legacy_trace_field diagnostics without letting them outrank req_ids.
- [x] AC-4: Resume validation returns explicit outcomes such as ok, confirmed_done, repairable_drift, stale_artifact, blocked_dependency, needs_user, invalid_artifact, or resume-blocked.
- [x] AC-5: Integration tests cover hallucination guards for wrong artifact path, wrong next task due to ID sorting, stale resume state, missing req_ids, worklog mismatch, and pipeline correction conflict.

### ~~FR-NODE-022 — Guarded workflow progress mutations~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-NODE-030]

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide guarded workflow progress mutations for plan checkbox display state, plan checklist display state, PM task status, JSONL append events, and repair records with dry-run, canonical journal keys and states, idempotency, stale guards, ownership checks, ordered multi-artifact writes, and explicit partial-failure reporting.

#### Rationale

The Kiwi planner research found that checkbox and task-status writes touch multiple workflow artifacts. A single boolean success flag is unsafe because partial writes can corrupt resume state or mislead the next agent.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Workflow mutation kinds include plan_checkbox_check, plan_checkbox_uncheck, plan_checklist_item_update, pm_task_status_update, pipeline_event_append, worklog_event_append, workflow_repair_record, and any required task-progress companion operations.
- [ ] AC-2: Workflow mutations enforce owner rules so child coders cannot directly mutate plan checkboxes when PM owns the display state.
- [ ] AC-3: Mutations validate current plan, sidecar, PM state, coder state, and worklog guards before writing and return stale_artifact or blocked_dependency without writing when guards fail.
- [ ] AC-4: Multi-artifact writes follow a documented order that records planned mutation state, appends planned or applying worklog entries, applies guarded display changes, records applied events, confirms final PM state, and records journalState as one of planned, applied, confirmed, failed, or skipped_dry_run.
- [ ] AC-5: Partial failures return the shared mutation envelope with journalKey computed by FR-NODE-028, journalState, idempotencyKey equal to journalKey unless a caller supplies a stricter compatible key, completedOperations, pendingOperations, pendingRepair, written false unless every required artifact write is confirmed, and a repair command hint.

### ~~FR-NODE-023 — Logical workflow deletion state~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-NODE-031]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, workflow, jsonl, deletion, tombstone |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md), [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide guarded logical deletion mutations for workflow artifacts that mark plan display records, PM task records, pipeline events, worklog events, and workflow repair records as `deleted` through append-only tombstone or repair records instead of physically deleting JSONL lines or Markdown history.

#### Rationale

Planner and JSONL artifacts are audit and resume evidence. Physical deletion can hide partial failures, break correction chains, and make the next agent hallucinate state. The safer deletion tool is a guarded state transition to `deleted` that keeps the original record available for audit while excluding it from default active-state reads.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Workflow logical-delete mutation kinds can mark supported workflow records as `deleted` effective state without physically removing the original JSONL line, Markdown checkbox line, PM state record, or repair record.
- [ ] AC-2: Each logical-delete write records the target record identity, deleted state, reason, owner, timestamp, journalKey or idempotency key, stale guards, and source artifact hashes needed to prove what was deleted.
- [ ] AC-3: Default workflow read tools treat logically deleted records as inactive while preserving audit metadata, and expose an explicit includeDeleted or equivalent option for read/list/tail inspection and repair.
- [ ] AC-4: Logical-delete mutations reject stale artifacts, invalid record IDs, forbidden owners, malformed JSONL, dependency-blocked writes, attempts to delete records that are not in a deletable class, tombstone-of-tombstone, correction-of-tombstone, and plain deletion of `CORRECTION` events unless a future restore requirement defines safe semantics.
- [ ] AC-5: Logical-delete mutations are idempotent: deleting an already deleted record returns a successful no-op mutation envelope without writing a duplicate tombstone unless the caller explicitly requests a new repair note.
- [ ] AC-6: Pipeline logical-delete handling must not add `deleted` to the pipeline `status` enum; it must represent deletion as `status=CORRECTION` plus `operation.kind="logical_delete"` or an equivalent tombstone/repair record, and apply logical-delete tombstones before normal correction chains so directly tombstoned records cannot be resurrected by correction-of-correction.
- [ ] AC-7: Tests cover logical delete, already-deleted no-op, non-deletable record rejection, stale hash rejection, invalid JSONL rejection, read filtering, includeDeleted inspection, tombstone/correction-of-tombstone rejection, `status=deleted` rejection, and partial-failure repair reporting.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core completed-work services must resolve a single canonical Completed Work Log write target, preferring docs/spec/05.completed-work.md when present and falling back to the legacy index table when absent, without bidirectional synchronization.

#### Rationale

The research recommends moving future completed-work rows out of 00.index.md to reduce token load while preserving backward compatibility and avoiding dual writes.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: listCompletedWork merges legacy and external completed-work rows only through an explicit source-aware read model and preserves each row source file and line.
- [ ] AC-2: addCompletedWork writes each new row to exactly one canonical target file: docs/spec/05.completed-work.md when present, otherwise docs/spec/00.index.md legacy table fallback.
- [ ] AC-3: addCompletedWork does not implicitly migrate historical completed-work rows as a hidden side effect.
- [ ] AC-4: A separate dry-run-capable migration operation or migration plan is required before moving historical rows from the index to the external file.
- [ ] AC-5: Summary generation exposes bounded completed-work data with total count, returned count, limit, and hasMore or continuation metadata when rows are omitted.

### ~~FR-NODE-025 — Requirement ID status cache~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-NODE-027]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, status-cache, requirement-id, performance, CAND-NODE-REQ-ID-STATUS-CACHE |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must maintain a derived kiwi/.status.json cache for Requirement ID allocation counters and SRS file fingerprints so add_requirement can avoid full workspace scans when the cache is fresh, while always falling back to canonical Markdown parsing when the cache is missing, stale, malformed, or unsafe.

#### Rationale

Doc230 found that ID generation scans workspace.records and add_requirement reparses before write. A derived status cache can reduce repeated registration cost in large projects without changing Markdown as the source of truth.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: kiwi/.status.json is explicitly derived state and is never treated as canonical requirement evidence or a replacement for docs/spec Markdown parsing.
- [ ] AC-2: A fresh cache stores next-ID counters per requirement type and scope prefix plus freshness fingerprints for docs/spec/00.index.md and registered scope SRS documents.
- [ ] AC-3: Missing, malformed, stale, or unsafe cache data causes add_requirement to fall back to the current full workspace parse and continue safely when canonical SRS Markdown is valid.
- [ ] AC-4: A generated ID obtained from the cache is still rechecked against canonical parsed Requirement records before any write occurs.
- [ ] AC-5: After a successful SRS mutation, SpecKiwi atomically regenerates or updates kiwi/.status.json; if cache write fails after the SRS mutation succeeds, the mutation may succeed with a structured warning.
- [ ] AC-6: Tests cover fresh cache use, stale cache fallback, corrupt cache fallback, raw Markdown edit drift, generated-ID collision after cache read, and cache write failure after successful mutation.

### ~~REL-NODE-004 — SRS mutation lock core~~ [DISCARDED → see REL-NODE-005]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, srs-lock, ignore-lock, concurrency, status-cache, CAND-NODE-SRS-MUTATION-LOCK |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core mutation services must use an SRS-only atomic lock file for writes to docs/spec, SRS rule files, index rollups, and SRS mutation cache files, while mirroring lock state into kiwi/.status.json for diagnostics without making the cache the lock authority.

#### Rationale

Doc230 adversarial review found that stdio MCP and CLI processes cannot rely on process-local memory for mutation exclusion. A separate lock file prevents concurrent SRS writes while keeping kiwi/.status.json disposable.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: The authoritative SRS mutation lock is the separate atomic lock file kiwi/.srs.lock, not kiwi/.status.json.
- [ ] AC-2: The lock applies only to SRS mutation operations that can write docs/spec, docs/rule when part of SRS mutation contracts, SRS index rollups, or SRS mutation cache files.
- [ ] AC-3: Read-only operations remain available while a non-stale SRS mutation lock is active.
- [ ] AC-4: A non-stale active lock causes mutation attempts to fail fast with structured SRS_LOCKED diagnostics including owner, operation, request ID, acquiredAt, expiresAt, and retry guidance.
- [ ] AC-5: Stale locks are recovered deterministically after expiry, and explicit lock bypass records diagnostics without disabling stale patch guards, generated-ID collision checks, table-cell safety, trace validation, or SRS validation.
- [ ] AC-6: Tests cover active lock denial, stale lock recovery, lock metadata, concurrent mutation attempts, process crash simulation, ignored-lock diagnostics, and lock state mirroring into kiwi/.status.json.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core completed-work services must resolve a single canonical Completed Work Log write target, preferring docs/spec/05.completed-work.md when present and falling back to the legacy index table when absent, without bidirectional synchronization.

#### Rationale

The research recommends moving future completed-work rows out of 00.index.md to reduce token load while preserving backward compatibility and avoiding dual writes.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: listCompletedWork merges legacy and external completed-work rows only through an explicit source-aware read model and preserves each row source file and line.

- [x] AC-3: addCompletedWork does not implicitly migrate historical completed-work rows as a hidden side effect.
- [x] AC-4: A separate dry-run-capable migration operation or migration plan is required before moving historical rows from the index to the external file.
- [x] AC-5: Summary generation exposes bounded completed-work data with total count, returned count, limit, and hasMore or continuation metadata when rows are omitted.

### FR-NODE-027 — Requirement ID status cache

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core requirement mutations must use kiwi/.status.json as an optional derived cache for Requirement ID counters and SRS mutation status, while preserving docs/spec/**/*.srs.md as the canonical source of truth and falling back to full workspace parsing whenever the cache is missing, malformed, stale, or unsafe.

#### Rationale

Doc230 found that add_requirement currently scans all Requirement Blocks to generate IDs, which is robust but can become costly in very large SRS repositories. A repository-local derived cache can reduce repeated full scans without weakening Markdown canonicality.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: kiwi/.status.json stores schemaVersion, generatedAt, source, specFingerprint data, idCounters keyed by requirement type and scope prefix, and mirrored SRS lock status for diagnostics.
- [x] AC-2: add_requirement validates cache schema, source, fingerprints, ID prefix mapping, and relevant SRS file freshness before using cached counters.
- [x] AC-3: If the cache is absent, malformed, stale, incomplete, or unsafe, add_requirement falls back to the existing full workspace parse and does not fail only because the cache is unusable.
- [x] AC-4: Before any write, the selected Requirement ID is rechecked against canonical parsed SRS records so discarded or existing IDs are never reused.
- [x] AC-5: After a successful SRS mutation, SpecKiwi regenerates or atomically updates kiwi/.status.json; cache write failure after a successful SRS mutation returns success with diagnostics because the cache is rebuildable.
- [x] AC-6: Tests cover valid-cache allocation, missing-cache fallback, malformed-cache fallback, stale-fingerprint fallback, final collision detection, atomic cache write, and cache-write warning behavior.

### REL-NODE-005 — SRS mutation lock core

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, srs-lock, ignore-lock, concurrency, status-cache, CAND-NODE-SRS-MUTATION-LOCK |
| Risk | high |

| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core mutation services must enforce SRS-only write exclusion with an authoritative atomic lock file at kiwi/.srs.lock, while mirroring lock state into kiwi/.status.json for diagnostics without making the derived cache the lock authority.

#### Rationale

Doc230 adversarial review found that stdio MCP and CLI processes cannot rely on process-local memory, and kiwi/.status.json must remain disposable. A separate atomic lock file prevents concurrent SRS writes without letting cache deletion clear an active lock.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The authoritative SRS mutation lock path is kiwi/.srs.lock, and kiwi/.status.json only mirrors lock state for diagnostics.
- [x] AC-2: The lock applies only to SRS mutations that can write docs/spec, docs/rule when part of an SRS mutation contract, SRS index rollups, or SRS mutation cache files.
- [x] AC-3: Read-only operations remain available while a non-stale SRS mutation lock is active.
- [x] AC-4: Lock acquisition uses single-winner atomic create semantics with no check-then-create race window; lock metadata is fully written and fsync-equivalent or platform-durable before the lock is considered acquired.
- [x] AC-5: A non-stale active lock causes SRS mutation attempts to fail fast with structured SRS_LOCKED diagnostics including owner, operation, requestId, acquiredAt, expiresAt, and retry guidance.
- [x] AC-6: Stale locks are recovered deterministically after expiry through a compare-and-replace or equivalent guarded replacement that rejects symlink locks, outside-root paths, malformed metadata, and concurrent replacement races.
- [x] AC-7: Explicit lock bypass records diagnostics without disabling stale patch guards, generated-ID collision checks, table-cell safety, trace validation, SRS validation, symlink checks, or inside-root checks.
- [x] AC-8: Tests cover active lock denial, stale lock recovery, single-winner concurrent acquisition, no check-then-create race, lock metadata durability, process crash simulation, ignored-lock diagnostics, symlink and outside-root rejection, and lock state mirroring into kiwi/.status.json.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core workflow services must define read-only diagnostic projection contracts for workflow doctor, workflow diff, workflow schema-check, and pipeline compact style views by composing the shared workflow artifact resolver, JSONL utilities, validator outcomes, and logical-delete effective-state rules without creating independent selectors or mutation paths.

#### Rationale

Doc230 Plus found these tools are useful only as compact read projections over existing resolver, JSONL, validator, and delete-state contracts. A core projection contract prevents CLI and MCP adapters from implementing divergent diagnosis or next-action selection logic.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Diagnostic projections return a compact envelope with projection kind, artifact references, source hashes or mtimes, diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, blocking state, and optional cursor metadata when the projection is tail-like.
- [x] AC-2: Projection outcome codes include invalid_plan_contract, unsupported_schema_version, task_state_conflict, repairable_drift, stale_artifact, blocked_dependency, invalid_artifact, deleted_record_filtered, and no_actionable_drift when applicable.
- [x] AC-3: Projection implementations consume FR-NODE-020, FR-NODE-021, REL-NODE-003, and FR-NODE-031 outputs and do not mutate workflow, pipeline, plan, PM, coder, worklog, or SRS artifacts.
- [x] AC-4: Pipeline compact projections apply logical-delete tombstones before normal corrections, exclude invalid, corrected, deleted, and meta records from default latest-state selection, and reject status=DELETED as invalid pipeline status.
- [x] AC-5: Tests cover doctor aggregation, normalized diff classes, fail-closed schema-check outcomes, compact pipeline latest-state calculation, raw-body omission by default, and parity of projection decisions with the underlying validator results.

### FR-NODE-028 — Canonical workflow journal identity and repair inspection

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, workflow, mutation, journal, repair |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230plus.md](../research/230plus.md), [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi workflow mutation services must use a canonical journal identity that includes workflow run identity, expose journal inspection as mutation evidence rather than a separate source of truth, and restrict repair application to recorded pendingRepair records with dry-run-first and stale-guard checks.

#### Rationale

Doc230 Plus found that existing workflow mutation requirements describe journalKey formulas that omit run_id, which can collide across repeated task IDs in different runs. The research also found that repair is useful only when it operates on explicit pendingRepair records instead of inferring fixes from plan display state.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The canonical workflow mutation journalKey is sha256(tool|run_id|task_id|req_id|canonicalJson(flat_args)), where req_id uses a documented empty stable token for non-requirement-scoped workflow operations.
- [x] AC-2: Canonical JSON for journalKey calculation is deterministic across CLI and MCP adapters, including stable object-key ordering, normalized absent optional fields, and exact treatment of task_id, req_id, and run_id.
- [x] AC-3: Core mutation responses expose journalKey, journalState, idempotencyKey, completedOperations, pendingOperations, pendingRepair, diagnostics, and diagnosticsSummary using the canonical identity from this requirement.
- [x] AC-4: Journal inspection reports planned, applied, confirmed, failed, and skipped_dry_run states as audit evidence only and never outranks plan sidecar, PM state, worklog, or validator source-of-truth precedence.
- [x] AC-5: Repair application is dry-run-first by default, applies only a recorded pendingRepair whose stale guards still match, and never infers repairs from unchecked plan boxes or deleted/corrected JSONL records alone.

### FR-NODE-029 — Read-only legacy workflow migration preview core

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, workflow, migration-preview, legacy, doc230plus |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |

| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230plus.md](../research/230plus.md), [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core workflow services must provide a read-only legacy workflow migration preview that classifies legacy planning and session artifacts, proposes current-format destinations, reports unsupported fields and data-loss risks, and guarantees no writes or current-state promotion during preview.

#### Rationale

Doc230 Plus found that current and legacy workflow artifacts can coexist, but existing resolver, JSONL, and validator requirements do not define a user-facing migration-preview contract. A read-only preview is useful for planning migration without treating legacy artifacts as authoritative runtime state.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The preview discovers legacy docs/plan, docs/plan/**, .snoworca/sessions, and .snoworca/sessions/** artifacts only through the shared workflow resolver and marks every returned source with legacy, confidence, source path, and source hash metadata.
- [x] AC-2: The preview returns proposed current-format destinations, unsupported fields, lossy transforms, target mismatch, path drift, schema or contract mismatch, and required manual decisions without writing files.
- [x] AC-3: The preview result always reports written false and does not move, delete, normalize, rewrite, compact, or append workflow, pipeline, plan, session, worklog, or SRS artifacts.
- [x] AC-4: Legacy artifacts identified by preview are never promoted into current workflow state unless a separate future migration-apply requirement and explicit user action exist.
- [x] AC-5: Tests cover legacy-only artifacts, mixed current and legacy artifacts, unsupported fields, target drift, source hash reporting, raw-body omission by default, includeBody opt-in, and written false under all preview outcomes.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide guarded workflow progress mutations for plan checkbox display state, plan checklist display state, PM task status, JSONL append events, and repair records with dry-run, the canonical FR-NODE-028 journal identity, idempotency, stale guards, ownership checks, ordered multi-artifact writes, and explicit partial-failure reporting.

#### Rationale

This requirement supersedes FR-NODE-022 to remove the obsolete journalKey formula that omitted run_id. Doc230 Plus showed run_id is necessary to avoid cross-run collisions for repeated task IDs.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Workflow mutation kinds include plan_checkbox_check, plan_checkbox_uncheck, plan_checklist_item_update, pm_task_status_update, pipeline_event_append, worklog_event_append, workflow_repair_record, and any required task-progress companion operations.
- [x] AC-2: Workflow mutations enforce owner rules so child coders cannot directly mutate plan checkboxes when PM owns the display state.
- [x] AC-3: Mutations validate current plan, sidecar, PM state, coder state, and worklog guards before writing and return stale_artifact or blocked_dependency without writing when guards fail.
- [x] AC-4: Multi-artifact writes follow a documented order that records planned mutation state, appends planned or applying worklog entries, applies guarded display changes, records applied events, confirms final PM state, and records journalState as one of planned, applied, confirmed, failed, or skipped_dry_run.
- [x] AC-5: Partial failures return the shared mutation envelope with journalKey computed by FR-NODE-028 as sha256(tool|run_id|task_id|req_id|canonicalJson(flat_args)), journalState, idempotencyKey equal to journalKey unless a caller supplies a stricter compatible key, completedOperations, pendingOperations, pendingRepair, written false unless every required artifact write is confirmed, and a repair command hint.

### FR-NODE-031 — Logical workflow deletion state with canonical mutation dependency

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, workflow, jsonl, deletion, tombstone |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |

| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md), [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/230plus.md](../research/230plus.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must provide guarded logical deletion mutations for workflow artifacts that mark plan display records, PM task records, pipeline events, worklog events, and workflow repair records as `deleted` through append-only tombstone or repair records instead of physically deleting JSONL lines or Markdown history, using the canonical FR-NODE-030 guarded mutation contract.

#### Rationale

This requirement supersedes FR-NODE-023 so active logical-delete work no longer depends on the discarded FR-NODE-022 mutation contract. The behavior remains the same, but the dependency is now the canonical Doc230 Plus mutation successor.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Workflow logical-delete mutation kinds can mark supported workflow records as `deleted` effective state without physically removing the original JSONL line, Markdown checkbox line, PM state record, or repair record.
- [x] AC-2: Each logical-delete write records the target record identity, deleted state, reason, owner, timestamp, canonical journalKey or idempotency key, stale guards, and source artifact hashes needed to prove what was deleted.
- [x] AC-3: Default workflow read tools treat logically deleted records as inactive while preserving audit metadata, and expose an explicit includeDeleted or equivalent option for read/list/tail inspection and repair.
- [x] AC-4: Logical-delete mutations reject stale artifacts, invalid record IDs, forbidden owners, malformed JSONL, dependency-blocked writes, attempts to delete records that are not in a deletable class, tombstone-of-tombstone, correction-of-tombstone, and plain deletion of CORRECTION events unless a future restore requirement defines safe semantics.
- [x] AC-5: Logical-delete mutations are idempotent: deleting an already deleted record returns a successful no-op mutation envelope without writing a duplicate tombstone unless the caller explicitly requests a new repair note.
- [x] AC-6: Pipeline logical-delete handling must not add `deleted` to the pipeline status enum; it must represent deletion as status=CORRECTION plus operation.kind="logical_delete" or an equivalent tombstone/repair record, and apply logical-delete tombstones before normal correction chains so directly tombstoned records cannot be resurrected by correction-of-correction.
- [x] AC-7: Tests cover logical delete, already-deleted no-op, non-deletable record rejection, stale hash rejection, invalid JSONL rejection, read filtering, includeDeleted inspection, tombstone/correction-of-tombstone rejection, status=deleted rejection, and partial-failure repair reporting.

### FR-NODE-032 — Merge-time Requirement ID collision repair core

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, requirement-id, collision-repair, merge, mutation |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi core must provide guarded diagnose, plan, and apply services for merge-time Requirement ID collisions caused by parallel worktree or branch SRS additions, while rejecting general renumbering and preserving Markdown SRS as the canonical source of truth.

#### Rationale

Doc230 found that kiwi/.status.json and generated-ID optimistic concurrency reduce collisions within one checkout but cannot prevent independent worktrees from generating the same ID before merge. Existing granular editing and lock requirements are prerequisites, not the repair workflow itself.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Diagnose is read-only and returns duplicate Requirement ID groups from REL-PARSE-003, repair readiness diagnostics, candidate replacement IDs, ambiguous inbound references, and Git merge provenance when reliable provenance metadata is available.
- [x] AC-2: Plan requires an explicit duplicateId plus keep and rename occurrences identified by filePath, headingLine, and blockHash or equivalent block identity, accepts either an explicit replacementId or allocationStrategy=next_available, and returns a dry-run multi-file patch plan without writing files.
- [x] AC-3: Apply uses the REL-NODE-005 SRS mutation lock, FR-NODE-017 mutation envelope, FR-NODE-027 allocation cache fallback, and a final canonical full-parse check that permits the selected duplicate group being repaired while rejecting stale occurrence identity, replacementId collisions, and unrelated duplicate groups unless the plan explicitly reports them as pending; ignoreLock bypasses only the SRS lock.
- [x] AC-4: Repair updates only the selected duplicate heading, the selected block's unambiguous self-reference rows, and explicitly mapped Trace Links or Completed Work Log Requirement IDs cells, while preserving Status, Stability, Acceptance Criteria, Verification Evidence, and free-text prose unless separate guarded mutations are invoked.
- [x] AC-5: Ambiguous inbound references, Completed Work Log rows without explicit mapping, research and analysis files, pipeline logs, evidence notes, Change Notes prose, and arbitrary Markdown text are report-only by default and never rewritten by automatic inference.
- [x] AC-6: Multi-file apply verifies all touched file snapshots before writing, records completed and pending operations, attempts rollback on partial failure, returns pendingRepair metadata when needed, and never reports written true unless every required write is confirmed.
- [x] AC-7: Tests cover same-file and cross-file duplicates, multi-occurrence duplicates, unresolved Git conflict markers, ambiguous references, completed-work references, stale snapshots, generated-ID races, active locks, ignoreLock lock-only bypass, idempotent reruns, and post-apply validate, summary, and links gates.

### FR-NODE-033 — Origin-aware record loading for mutation routing

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL expose internal requirement loaders (loadRecord and loadRecordWithWorkspace) that accept optional origin ("body" | "step") and stepName parameters so mutation routing can distinguish the body-scope copy of a requirement ID from a same-ID copy living in a step partition. When origin and stepName are omitted, the loaders SHALL resolve the body-scope record for the given ID, preserving existing behavior. When origin="step" and a stepName are supplied, the loaders SHALL resolve the matching step-partition record for that ID, tag the returned record with origin="step" and the supplied stepName, and surface the step file's on-disk path so callers can route the mutation to the correct copy.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a requirement ID that exists in both the body scope and a step partition, calling loadRecord with no origin argument returns the body copy: the returned record's origin is not "step", its title is the body copy's title, and its filePath does not contain the step directory path (docs/spec/steps/).
- [ ] AC-2: Given the same ID present in both partitions, calling loadRecord with origin="step" and a matching stepName returns the step copy: the returned record has origin="step", stepName equal to the supplied step name, the step copy's title, and a filePath under docs/spec/steps/<stepName>/.
- [ ] AC-3: Existing no-argument callers remain backward compatible: both loadRecord and loadRecordWithWorkspace, called without origin/stepName, resolve the same body record that parseWorkspace returns for that ID (identical filePath and title) and never return a record tagged origin="step".

### FR-NODE-034 — Successor trace search over union records to prevent slot regression

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL resolve the successor annotation of a requirement's discard/draft heading marker by searching the full union of parsed requirement records (SRS body records together with workspace step records) for incoming trace rows that reference the target requirement. Incoming `supersedes` and `conflicts_with` traces SHALL be searched independently per relation and returned in a deterministic FIRST order (by source file path, then by line). The derived successor slot SHALL name the first matching superseding requirement together with a count of the remaining matches, so that a marker never regresses to its default form when qualifying traces exist in any record origin.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Incoming-trace search over the record set returns exactly the trace rows that reference the target requirement with the requested (type, relation) pair — e.g. Requirement/supersedes — carrying the source requirement's ID; rows whose type or relation differ (e.g. Code/supersedes, Requirement/depends_on) are excluded, and when no qualifying row exists the result is empty.
- [ ] AC-2: Matching rows are returned in a deterministic FIRST order (lexicographically by source file path, then ascending by line number), and each relation is searched in isolation so a conflicts_with query never returns supersedes rows and a supersedes query never returns conflicts_with rows.
- [ ] AC-3: The successor slot is derived from the ordered matches such that an empty match set yields no slot (undefined → default marker), a single match yields that requirement's ID with count 0, and N matches yield the first match's ID with count N-1.

### FR-NODE-035 — updateStatus verified-regression discard guard with explicit override

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL protect requirements against accidental regression by refusing to transition a protected requirement to `discarded` unless the caller supplies an explicit `confirmDiscardVerified=true` override. A requirement is protected when its Status is `verified`, its Stability is `frozen` or `stable`, or its Status is `implemented` while it carries at least one non-empty verification evidence reference. When the override is absent, `updateStatus` SHALL return a `MUTATION_DENIED` failure with an informative message and SHALL NOT modify the SRS document. When the override is present, the discard transition SHALL proceed and persist Status=discarded. The guard runs before any patch is constructed so a denied discard leaves the document byte-identical.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Transitioning a requirement whose Status is `verified` to `discarded` without `confirmDiscardVerified` returns a failure with error code `MUTATION_DENIED`.
- [ ] AC-2: Transitioning a requirement whose Stability is `stable` or `frozen` to `discarded` without `confirmDiscardVerified` returns a failure with error code `MUTATION_DENIED`.
- [ ] AC-3: Transitioning a requirement whose Status is `implemented` and that carries at least one verification evidence entry to `discarded` without `confirmDiscardVerified` returns a failure with error code `MUTATION_DENIED`.
- [ ] AC-4: Transitioning a verified requirement to `discarded` while supplying both a `reason` and `confirmDiscardVerified=true` succeeds (ok=true) and the persisted spec document shows `| Status | discarded |`.
- [ ] AC-5: A denied discard transition returns error code `MUTATION_DENIED` with a non-empty message and leaves the SRS document byte-for-byte unchanged (no partial mutation).

### FR-NODE-036 — Semantic requirement content-hash under frozen fpv=1 protocol

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a pure `computeSemanticSha(record)` function that returns a deterministic 40-character lowercase SHA-1 hex digest of a requirement's semantic content, bound to a frozen protocol version tag (`fpv=1`). The hash SHALL be computed only over the normalized requirement text, the acceptance-criteria identity and text (excluding checked state), the canonical Scope taken from the top-level `record.scope` field, and metadata excluding the `Status` and `Stability` keys. It SHALL exclude Trace Links, Verification Evidence, Change Notes, and the record-level status/stability fields, so that equal semantic content yields an equal hash regardless of non-semantic changes. The `fpv=1` tag SHALL be part of the hashed input so the algorithm can only evolve by bumping the protocol version rather than silently changing previously documented digests.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Mutating the Trace Links, Verification Evidence, or Change Notes of an otherwise-identical record MUST leave computeSemanticSha unchanged (these sections are excluded from the hash input).
- [ ] AC-2: Changing the Status or Stability metadata keys, or the record-level status/stability fields, MUST leave the hash unchanged; changing the canonical top-level Scope (record.scope) MUST change the hash. Scope contributes to the hash only through record.scope, with no synthetic 'Scope' metadata key required.
- [ ] AC-3: Flipping an acceptance criterion's checked/unchecked state MUST leave the hash unchanged, while changing acceptance-criterion text MUST change the hash.
- [ ] AC-4: Requirement text and AC text MUST be normalized before hashing — CRLF converted to LF, per-line trailing whitespace stripped, internal whitespace runs collapsed to a single space, and outer whitespace trimmed — so two records differing only by these normalizable forms MUST hash identically.
- [ ] AC-5: A frozen fpv=1 test vector MUST produce a stable, documented 40-character lowercase SHA-1 hex digest returned identically on every call (pure/deterministic), and a vector differing only in the requirement text MUST produce a different digest; the documented digest pins the fpv=1 algorithm so any intentional change to the hashing contract requires bumping to fpv=2 with a new vector rather than editing the frozen value (documented frozen digest: b9206c826e1975cc676bdc96610548cfd0121c3d).
