### FR-NODE-037 — compareReqId raw-byte ordering and computeBlastRadius depends_on closure utility

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide two pure utility functions in the mutation records core (src/core/mutation/records.ts). compareReqId SHALL order two Requirement IDs by raw byte (code-unit) comparison, returning a negative number when the first ID is less, a positive number when it is greater, and zero when they are equal, so callers can deterministically select the minimum-side block during single-row normalization. computeBlastRadius SHALL return the transitive depends_on closure reachable within at most two hops from a given seed set, including the seeds themselves, and SHALL stop traversal at requirements whose Stability is frozen or stable. Both functions SHALL be pure: they perform no I/O and do not mutate the input records or their trace links.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: compareReqId returns a sign determined by raw byte (code-unit) comparison of the two REQ-IDs — a value less than 0 when the first ID sorts before the second, greater than 0 when it sorts after, and exactly 0 for identical IDs; ordering is by raw bytes so an uppercase ID (e.g. FR-ARCH-001) sorts before its lowercase form (FR-arch-001), and the result is antisymmetric (swapping the operands flips the sign).
- [ ] AC-2: computeBlastRadius returns the transitive depends_on closure bounded to at most 2 hops from the seed set — the seed and its hop-1 and hop-2 depends_on dependencies are included, while a hop-3 dependency is excluded by the 2-hop bound.
- [ ] AC-3: computeBlastRadius stops traversal at requirements whose Stability is frozen or stable — such a node is itself included in the closure (it is the edge where traversal stops), but its outgoing depends_on edges are not followed, so its further dependencies are excluded.
- [ ] AC-4: computeBlastRadius is pure and side-effect-free — repeated calls with the same inputs return an equal closure, and it does not mutate the input records array or any record's trace links.

### FR-NODE-038 — add_compatibility_check mutation with dedup, frozen, and liveness guards and bidirectional semanticSha pins

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an add_compatibility_check mutation that records two requirements as mutually compatibility-checked by inserting a single normalized `checked_compatible` Trace Links row on the compareReqId-minimum requirement of the pair. The written row SHALL carry the peer (compareReqId-maximum) requirement's bare REQ-ID as its Reference, and its Notes SHALL pin the current semanticSha of both the self (min-side) and peer (max-side) endpoints so a later content change on either endpoint can be detected. The mutation SHALL be order-independent — the same pair always normalizes to the same min-side block regardless of argument order — and SHALL be guarded by endpoint liveness, self-side frozen-block protection, per-pair deduplication, and a self-pair rejection, writing only a bare live REQ-ID Reference that passes trace-target validation.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given two live requirements A and B, add_compatibility_check succeeds and inserts exactly one `checked_compatible` Trace Links row across the whole workspace; the row resides on the compareReqId-minimum block (self), its Reference equals the peer (max-side) REQ-ID, and its Notes contain both the self and the peer requirements' current semanticSha values. This holds regardless of the order the two IDs are passed.
- [ ] AC-2: A second add_compatibility_check for the same requirement pair (in either argument order) is rejected (result.ok === false) and the workspace is left with exactly one row (per-pair dedup).
- [ ] AC-3: add_compatibility_check is rejected (result.ok === false) when the compareReqId-minimum (self) block has Stability=frozen; the scope SRS document is left byte-for-byte unchanged and no compatibility row is written.
- [ ] AC-4: add_compatibility_check is rejected (result.ok === false) when either endpoint has Status=discarded, or has Stability=deprecated, or does not exist in the workspace.
- [ ] AC-5: The written Reference is a bare live REQ-ID (matching ^[A-Z]+-[A-Z]+-\d+$); after the write the workspace validates with zero SRS-E012 (trace target does not exist) diagnostics.
- [ ] AC-6: AC-6 (self-pair guard): add_compatibility_check with aReqId === bReqId is rejected (result.ok === false) and leaves the SRS document unmodified with no row written.

### FR-NODE-039 — refresh_compatibility_check and revoke_compatibility_check mutations

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide two order-independent mutations, refresh_compatibility_check and revoke_compatibility_check, that operate on the single normalized `checked_compatible` Trace Links row placed by add_compatibility_check (FR-NODE-022) on the compareReqId-minimum (self) block of a requirement pair. refresh_compatibility_check SHALL locate that single min-side row, recompute both endpoints' current semanticSha pins, and replace the row in place so a content change on either endpoint is re-pinned; revoke_compatibility_check SHALL remove that row entirely. Both mutations SHALL normalize the (aReqId, bReqId) pair to the same min-side target regardless of argument order, SHALL return NOT_FOUND when no matching row exists, and SHALL be rejected without any partial edit when two or more matching rows are present.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: refresh_compatibility_check locates the single min-side checked_compatible row for the pair and replaces it in place (via replaceLine), leaving exactly one row that still resides on the compareReqId-minimum block and still references the peer (max-side) REQ-ID. After a material content change on an endpoint, the refreshed Notes cell differs from the originally recorded one, parses under the canonical SRS-MD-Rules §23.5 token grammar with fpv=fpv1, and its self/peer pins equal the current semanticSha of the min-side and peer records respectively.
- [ ] AC-2: refresh_compatibility_check returns an error with code NOT_FOUND when no matching checked_compatible row exists for the pair (it was never checked), and writes nothing.
- [ ] AC-3: refresh_compatibility_check is rejected when two or more matching checked_compatible rows exist on the min-side block (ambiguous target), leaving both rows untouched with no partial edit.
- [ ] AC-4: revoke_compatibility_check removes the single min-side checked_compatible row via a range replacement, leaving the block with zero checked_compatible rows; it succeeds regardless of the order in which the pair's two REQ-IDs are passed.

### FR-NODE-040 — list_dirty_edges read path with clean whitelist gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a `listDirtyEdges(root, options?)` read path that performs its own fresh workspace parse from the given ProjectRoot and enumerates every `checked_compatible` compatibility edge as a `DirtyEdge` carrying the holder block's bare REQ-ID (`self`), the referenced bare REQ-ID (`peer`), and a `classification` of `clean`, `dirty`, `orphaned`, or `missing`. Classification SHALL follow a strict whitelist gate: an edge is `clean` only when every required condition holds, and any single failing condition SHALL flip it to `dirty` with no blacklist shortcut. An edge whose referenced peer no longer exists SHALL be classified `orphaned`, and an edge whose canonical source/holder endpoint is absent SHALL be classified `missing`. An optional `target` MAY restrict the holder set considered.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: An edge is classified `clean` only when all whitelist conditions hold together — exactly one row exists for the unordered pair, the Notes cell parses under the compatibility-notes grammar, fpv equals `fpv1`, the row's `self` pin equals the current semanticSha of the holder endpoint, the `peer` pin equals the current semanticSha of the peer endpoint, and both endpoints are cache-live (present, status != discarded, stability != deprecated).
- [ ] AC-2: An edge that fails one or more clean-gate conditions (e.g. a well-formed but stale/non-current `self` pin) is classified `dirty`; the whitelist is the only path to `clean`, so a failing-condition edge is never silently reported as `clean`.
- [ ] AC-3: An edge whose referenced peer was deleted (peer REQ-ID absent from the workspace) is classified `orphaned`, and an edge whose canonical source/holder (compareReqId-minimum) endpoint is absent is classified `missing`.
- [ ] AC-4: `listDirtyEdges` accepts only a ProjectRoot (not a pre-parsed workspace) and parses the workspace from disk itself, so on-disk compatibility edges are surfaced without any externally supplied parse.

### FR-NODE-041 — edit_acceptance_criteria core mutation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a core SRS mutation that edits the prose text of a single targeted acceptance criterion on a requirement block, addressed by requirement id and acceptance-criterion id. The mutation SHALL replace only the criterion's prose while preserving its `AC-N:` id prefix and its checked/unchecked checkbox state, and SHALL leave all sibling criteria and every other requirement section (Requirement statement, Trace Links, etc.) byte-for-byte unchanged. Because the supplied text is written verbatim onto the acceptance-criterion line, the mutation SHALL reject text that is empty, exceeds the maximum length, or contains newline/control characters with a USAGE error and no file write.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Editing a targeted acceptance criterion replaces its prose text (the old text is gone and the new text is present), while its `AC-N:` id prefix and checkbox are preserved and every untargeted sibling criterion is left byte-for-byte unchanged.
- [ ] AC-2: Editing an acceptance criterion's text preserves its existing checkbox state (a checked `[x]` entry stays `[x]` and does not revert to `[ ]`), and unedited sibling criteria retain their own checkbox state.
- [ ] AC-3: Editing an acceptance criterion leaves the requirement's Requirement statement section and its Trace Links section (heading and table) byte-for-byte unchanged.
- [ ] AC-4: AC-4 (FND-005 hardening): Text that is empty, exceeds the maximum length (2000 UTF-16 code units), or contains a newline, carriage return, or control character is rejected with a USAGE error and the document is left completely unchanged (no write).

### FR-NODE-042 — claim_step mutation with write-skew two-stage gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a claim_step mutation that records a work-in-progress step by declaring the scope (TouchesScope) and requirement IDs (TouchesReq) it touches, appending a row to docs/spec/steps/state.md. Before writing, the mutation SHALL screen the claim against the active steps already recorded in state.md using a two-stage intersection gate: a direct shared-requirement intersection is an unforceable HARD-BLOCK (STEP_DIRECT_CONFLICT), while an intersection that arises only transitively through the depends_on closure is a SOFT-BLOCK (STEP_OVERLAP) that the force flag may override by pinning an overlaps marker. The mutation SHALL additionally refuse a claim whose supersede target's requirement is verified or frozen (STEP_SUPERSEDE_PROTECTED). Whenever any block is raised the mutation SHALL write nothing, and when the depends_on closure is unavailable it SHALL degrade to a 1-hop direct hard-block while treating transitive overlaps as advisory.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: A claim with no conflicting active step succeeds (ok=true) and appends a pipe-delimited, parseable row to docs/spec/steps/state.md that carries the declared step name, TouchesScope, and TouchesReq.
- [ ] AC-2: A claim whose TouchesReq directly intersects an active step's TouchesReq is rejected with error code STEP_DIRECT_CONFLICT even when force=true; no row for the challenger is written and state.md is unchanged.
- [ ] AC-3: A claim that intersects an active step only transitively (via the depends_on closure) is soft-blocked with error code STEP_OVERLAP; the same claim with force=true succeeds, writes the step row, and pins an overlaps marker referencing the overlapping incumbent step.
- [ ] AC-4: A claim whose supersede target's requirement is verified OR frozen (each disjunct independently) is rejected with STEP_SUPERSEDE_PROTECTED and writes nothing, whereas a supersede target that is neither verified nor frozen is allowed and the step row is written.
- [ ] AC-5: When the depends_on closure is unavailable, a transitive-only intersection degrades to advisory so the claim proceeds (ok=true, no STEP_OVERLAP) and its row is written, while a 1-hop direct conflict still hard-blocks with STEP_DIRECT_CONFLICT.

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an `update_step_state` mutation that updates an existing step row in `docs/spec/steps/state.md` in place, identified by its Step column value. The mutation SHALL rewrite the row's Status, DependsOn, and Updated fields as requested while leaving other rows and columns untouched. The Status field SHALL only accept a value in {active, merging, merged, abandoned}; any other value SHALL be rejected without writing. When the targeted step row does not exist, the mutation SHALL fail with a NOT_FOUND error and write nothing.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given an existing step row, when update_step_state is called with a Status in {active, merging, merged, abandoned}, then the row's Status cell is rewritten to that value and the mutation succeeds; when called with a Status outside that set, the mutation fails (ok=false) and the state.md file is left byte-for-byte unchanged.
- [ ] AC-2: Given an existing step row, when update_step_state is called with a dependsOn value, then the row's DependsOn cell is rewritten to that value and the Updated cell is refreshed to today's date in YYYY-MM-DD form (no longer the previously stored value), and the mutation succeeds.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a step name that does not match any row, when update_step_state is called, then the mutation fails with error code NOT_FOUND and the state.md file is left byte-for-byte unchanged.

### FR-NODE-044 — Read-only list_steps query with topological ordering, cycle detection, and step advisories

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL expose a read-only `listSteps(root, options?)` query that fresh-parses `docs/spec/steps/state.md` (FR-PARSE-023 columns: Step, Status, DependsOn, TouchesScope, TouchesReq, Created, Updated) and returns `{ steps, advisories, cycle }`. The `steps` SHALL be returned in a valid Kahn topological order that honours the DependsOn edges, placing each depended-on step before any step that depends on it; when the DependsOn graph contains a cycle the query SHALL set `cycle` true and surface a STEP_CYCLE advisory rather than emitting a silent partial order. The query SHALL also emit advisory-only diagnostics from the STEP_* namespace — STEP_ORPHAN for a DependsOn edge pointing at a non-existent step, STEP_SUPERSEDE_PROTECTED for a step superseding a verified/frozen requirement, and STEP_DRIFT for a non-active (merged) step still touching a requirement that has since become verified/frozen. Requirement protection SHALL be judged from the canonical body requirement record, so a same-id step-origin copy must never shadow a protected body requirement and suppress an advisory.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given steps whose DependsOn edges are declared out of topological order in state.md (e.g. rows in order c->b, a, b->a), listSteps returns cycle=false and every step present, with each depended-on step positioned strictly before the step that depends on it (a before b before c) — a valid Kahn order, not insertion order.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a DependsOn cycle (e.g. x depends on y and y depends on x), listSteps sets cycle=true and includes a STEP_CYCLE advisory in the advisories list instead of silently producing a partial order.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a step that supersedes a verified requirement, a DependsOn edge pointing at a non-existent step, and a merged step still touching a now-verified requirement, listSteps emits STEP_SUPERSEDE_PROTECTED, STEP_ORPHAN, and STEP_DRIFT advisories respectively; the STEP_ORPHAN advisory references the dangling step and/or the missing dependency name.
- [ ] AC-4: AC-3 (protection precedence): When a same-id step-origin requirement copy is unprotected (planned/evolving) but the body requirement of that same id is verified/frozen, STEP_SUPERSEDE_PROTECTED is still emitted for a step superseding that id — protection is judged from the body record, so the unprotected step copy does not shadow it and suppress the advisory.

### FR-NODE-045 — supersede_requirement strict two-call mutation with guards and compatibility invalidation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a supersede_requirement mutation that replaces an existing requirement (oldId) with a newly minted successor through a strict, ordered two-call sequence: first add_requirement (id unspecified, minting the successor with a `supersedes oldId` trace row and capturing the newly minted newId), then a hardened updateStatus(oldId, "discarded"). Before performing any mutation it SHALL enforce self-reference, reverse-direction-duplicate, and N>1 successor-ambiguity guards, and a rejected supersede SHALL leave the workspace byte-for-byte unchanged. The discard call SHALL honor the verified-regression exit guard, journal resumption SHALL be idempotent (no duplicate Change Notes row), and after a successful supersede the mutation SHALL invalidate the oldId endpoint's `checked_compatible` rows via revoke_compatibility_check.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a non-protected (evolving) supersede target oldId, supersedeRequirement returns ok, executes add_requirement then discards oldId in that strict order, surfaces a newId that is a string distinct from oldId, the newly minted successor exists and carries a `supersedes oldId` trace row, and oldId's status becomes "discarded".
- [ ] AC-2: supersedeRequirement rejects, before any document mutation (the SRS file is left byte-identical), each of: (a) a self-referential supersede where successorId equals oldId, (b) a reverse-direction duplicate where the candidate successor already has an outgoing `supersedes oldId` edge, and (c) an ambiguous supersede where oldId already has more than one existing successor; each rejection returns ok=false with a non-empty error message.
- [ ] AC-3: When oldId is a protected (verified) requirement, the hardened T2 discard denies the supersede with error code MUTATION_DENIED and leaves oldId verified unless the caller supplies both a reason and confirmDiscardVerified=true; with that explicit override the supersede proceeds and discards oldId.
- [ ] AC-4: Re-running supersede on an oldId that is already discarded with an existing successor (journal resumption) is idempotent: it does not append a duplicate discard Change Notes row to oldId (change-notes count is unchanged) and oldId's status remains "discarded".
- [ ] AC-5: After a successful supersede, every `checked_compatible` trace row touching oldId (whether oldId holds the row or is the referenced peer) is revoked, so that zero compatibility rows referencing oldId survive.

### FR-NODE-046 — promote_step_requirement mutation with reservation-unique verbatim id insertion

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a promote_step_requirement mutation that promotes a step-scoped requirement into a target body scope by inserting the step's pre-minted canonical Requirement ID and its requirement block verbatim, without auto-generating or altering the ID. Before insertion the system SHALL verify that the ID is globally unique against the reservation view of existing body requirements, and SHALL deny the promotion — leaving the target scope document unchanged — when the ID already exists. On success the promoted requirement SHALL appear in the target body scope carrying the step's exact ID.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a step requirement whose pre-minted ID does not collide with any body requirement, promoting it into the target scope succeeds (result ok) and the target body scope subsequently contains a requirement carrying the step's exact ID verbatim — the ID heading is present in the on-disk body scope file and the parsed body record has origin 'body'.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a step requirement whose ID already exists as a body requirement in the reservation view, the promotion is denied (result not ok, with a non-empty error message) and the target body scope document is left byte-identical to its prior content, with no duplicated ID heading.
- [ ] AC-3: The promoted requirement's returned ID equals the step's original pre-minted ID and is never replaced by an auto-generated next sequential scope ID (e.g., a non-sequential FR-ARCH-777 remains FR-ARCH-777 rather than being minted as the next slot FR-ARCH-002, which is confirmed absent from the body).

### FR-NODE-047 — MultiFileCommit four-phase engine with durable merge-journal crash recovery

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL commit a single requirement-level change that spans multiple SRS files (e.g. a step file and a body file) as one atomic, all-or-nothing operation executed through a fixed four-phase pipeline: render all final contents, stale-check every file, write temporary files with backups, then batch-rename as the final durable phase. Before the rename phase the system SHALL persist a durable merge-journal recording each rename (from/to paths, a sha256 content hash, and a backup) so that a merge interrupted by a crash is detected by the next merge and recovered by converging the workspace to either the fully committed (rolled-forward) or the fully pre-merge (rolled-back) state, never a partial application. The system SHALL exclude state.md from any commit plan.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: A commit touching a step file and a body file executes the phases in the exact order [render, stale-check, write-tmp, rename] with rename as the last phase; on success both target files contain their committed content and no temporary (.tmp) files remain in the workspace.
- [ ] AC-2: Before the rename phase begins, a durable merge-journal exists on disk containing one rename entry per committed file (two entries for two files), each entry carrying string from and to paths, a 64-hex-character sha256 hash of the new content, and a backup path.
- [ ] AC-3: An interrupted merge (crash mid-rename) leaves a journal with status 'half-applied' on disk; the next call to recoverMerge detects and recovers it with outcome 'rolled-forward' (both files committed) or 'rolled-back' (both files at original content), then clears the journal. If completing the outstanding rename fails with a real (non-ENOENT) error, recovery rolls the already-applied renames back to pre-merge content from the backups (outcome 'rolled-back'). A roll-back is terminal: a retried recovery after a roll-back never re-applies the outstanding rename forward, and a journal already marked 'rolled-back' resumes as a roll-back (never forward), leaving the workspace fully at pre-merge state.
- [ ] AC-4: The journal is written atomically via a temp-then-rename step, resumption/recovery is idempotent (recovering when nothing is outstanding returns recovered=false as a no-op), and a partial stale condition aborts all-or-nothing: if any planned file changed after planning, commit rejects with error code STALE_PATCH and no planned file is modified by the commit.
- [ ] AC-5: state.md is never part of a MultiFileCommit plan; a commit whose files include state.md rejects with an error mentioning 'state.md', leaves state.md untouched, and plannedPaths() excludes state.md.

### FR-NODE-048 — Merge-journal append-log with resume skip

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL extend the multi-file merge-commit journal so that every planned rename is recorded durably before it is performed, capturing the rename source and target, the sha256 hash of the final content to be renamed in, and the requirement id driving the commit (recorded both on the journal as a whole and on each individual entry). Each rename SHALL be marked as applied durably immediately after it succeeds, so that a subsequent resume of an interrupted merge reads the journal, skips every operation already recorded as applied, replays only the outstanding renames, and then clears the journal — converging any half-applied merge to the fully committed state without re-performing completed renames.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: When a MultiFileCommit commits with a requirementId, the on-disk merge-journal (readable via readMergeJournal) records one entry per rename; each entry exposes string from/to paths, a sha256 field matching /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/, and a per-entry requirementId equal to the commit's requirementId, and the journal as a whole also carries that requirementId. The recorded sha256 for each target equals the sha256 of the actual final file content that is renamed into that target.
- [ ] AC-2: When a merge is interrupted mid-rename (the first rename performed and durably marked applied, the second aborted by a crash before running), the stranded journal marks exactly the applied rename(s) as applied === true (one applied, one not). A resuming merge (MultiFileCommit.resume) re-performs only the not-yet-applied rename(s) — invoking onRename exactly once for the single outstanding target — reports committed === true, leaves both files in their committed content, and clears the journal so readMergeJournal returns null afterward.
- [ ] AC-3: Journal entries are appended durably before their corresponding rename runs and in order: at the instant each rename executes, the on-disk journal already contains an entry for that exact target that is not yet marked applied, and every rename that completed before it is already durably marked applied (the count of prior applied entries equals the number of renames already performed). A resume with no journal present (already cleared) reports committed === true without performing any rename.

### FR-NODE-049 — diff_steps four-way classification of step records against body keyed on semanticSha

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a `diffSteps` read operation that re-parses the workspace from a `ProjectRoot` (rather than accepting a pre-parsed workspace) and classifies every step-origin requirement record against its body-origin counterpart, using `computeSemanticSha` as the equality key. Each reported entry SHALL carry the bare requirement id, exactly one of four classifications — `NEW`, `UPDATE`, `CONFLICT-PARTIAL`, or `CONFLICT-FULL-GUARDED` — the step-side semantic hash, and (when a body counterpart exists) the body-side semantic hash. A step record whose `computeSemanticSha` equals its body counterpart's SHALL be treated as unchanged and omitted from the result. When multiple step files touch the same id, the later step (last in discovery order) SHALL be the one diffed against the body. An optional `stepName` SHALL narrow the diff to a single step file.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: diffSteps SHALL use computeSemanticSha as the equality key. A step record that normalizes to the same semanticSha as its body counterpart (e.g. differing only in whitespace) SHALL NOT appear as a diff entry, while a step record whose semanticSha actually differs from the body SHALL be reported as a non-NEW change.
- [ ] AC-2: A requirement id that exists only in a step file (no body counterpart) SHALL be classified NEW; an id whose body counterpart exists but whose step content differs non-conflictingly SHALL be classified UPDATE.
- [ ] AC-3: A conflicting step change SHALL be classified CONFLICT-PARTIAL when the body endpoint is unprotected, and CONFLICT-FULL-GUARDED when the body endpoint is protected — where a protected endpoint is status=verified, stability=frozen, stability=stable, or status=implemented with verification evidence (the updateStatus EXIT guard surface).
- [ ] AC-4: For draft/evolving same-rank peers, when two step files touch the same id the later step (last in discovery order) SHALL win, so the reported stepSha equals the later step's computeSemanticSha and not the earlier step's.

### FR-NODE-050 — Work Mode Model with Fail-Open Get and Persisting Set over state.md

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a work-mode model over the step-state document docs/spec/steps/state.md, where the current work mode is one of {sdd, vibe, wait} recorded in a top-of-file metadata block (a Mode: line, and for vibe an Active Task: line) above the step-state table. The system SHALL expose getWorkMode, which returns the persisted Mode and, when the mode is vibe, the persisted Active Task name. getWorkMode SHALL be fail-open: an absent or invalid state.md (missing file, missing or malformed Mode, or a Mode value outside {sdd, vibe, wait}) SHALL yield Mode=wait rather than throwing. The system SHALL expose setWorkMode, which persists the given Mode back into the metadata block and, when the mode is vibe, persists the given Active Task name alongside it.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: getWorkMode returns the Mode persisted in the state.md metadata block; for a non-vibe mode (e.g. sdd) Active Task is absent, and for Mode=vibe it also returns the persisted Active Task name.
- [ ] AC-2: getWorkMode returns Mode=wait without throwing when state.md is absent, and likewise returns Mode=wait when the persisted Mode value is outside {sdd, vibe, wait} (fail-open).
- [ ] AC-3: setWorkMode reports success and rewrites the Mode value in the state.md metadata block so that a subsequent getWorkMode reads the new Mode back.
- [ ] AC-4: setWorkMode with Mode=vibe reports success and persists both the Mode: vibe line and the Active Task: <name> line into the metadata block, and both values round-trip through getWorkMode.

### FR-NODE-051 — speckiwi init project bootstrap: SRS scaffold and idempotent agent-instruction block management

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a `speckiwi init` project-bootstrap operation (`initProject`) that, given a resolved repository root, scaffolds the SpecKiwi SRS layout: the `docs/spec/` and `docs/rule/` directories, the bundled authoring rules documents named from the shipped rules-version constants, an index (`docs/spec/00.index.md`) carrying the Target Map plus the Status Summary, Requirement Type Summary, Completed Work Log, and Cross-scope Dependencies sections in a minimal empty state (empty Active Target and no seeded target row), an appendix (`docs/spec/90.appendix.md`) with report-path guidance, and a scope SRS document when the project holds none. The operation SHALL install the managed SpecKiwi SRS workflow instruction block (current version heading through its end marker) into both `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, reporting each created file. Instruction installation SHALL be idempotent and delimiter-aware: it SHALL replace an existing managed block only when that block is a properly end-marked versioned block or a recognized legacy unversioned block, and SHALL otherwise leave user prose mentions, marker-less headings, and unmanaged local sections intact while appending the current block. Optional `target` and `scope` inputs SHALL be reflected in the generated index (as a planned Target Map row and scope-map entry) and in the created scope SRS document.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Running init against an empty git repo (from an unrelated external cwd) returns ok and scaffolds the full layout: `created` includes the two agent files; the installed rules document carries the canonical rules header (`SRS-MD Authoring Rules`, `| Document ID | SRS-MD-RULES |`); `docs/spec/00.index.md` contains the Target Map, an empty Active Target row (`| Active Target |  |`), the `## 5. Status Summary`, `## 6. Requirement Type Summary`, `## 7. Completed Work Log`, and `## 8. Cross-scope Dependencies` sections, the completed-work-log table header, and does NOT contain a seeded target row (`| v1.0.0 | release | active | Initial target |`); `docs/spec/90.appendix.md` contains report-path guidance (`Report Paths`, `--report <path>`, `reportPaths`, `SRS-W024`); and both agent files contain the managed workflow block whose heading is the shipped heading prefix followed by the shipped instruction version, carrying the Status/Stability, TDD, Next-target-authoring, and Current-work-status instruction lines, bounded by the shipped end marker.
- [x] AC-2: Init is idempotent — invoking it twice on the same root returns ok and leaves exactly one managed heading and exactly one end marker in each agent file, both matching the shipped constants (no duplicated snippets).
- [x] AC-3: Init replaces stale managed blocks — an existing end-marked versioned block carrying a lower version than the shipped one, and a legacy unversioned block, are removed and rewritten to the current version, while unmanaged local sections (e.g. `# Local Section`) are preserved.
- [x] AC-4: Init does not treat marker-less content as a managed block — an inline or prose mention of a versioned heading and a bare versioned heading that lacks the end marker are left intact, and the current managed block is appended separately rather than overwriting them.
- [x] AC-5: Init does not pair a malformed (marker-less) managed-style heading with an unrelated later end marker — the malformed heading and all intervening local sections are preserved, and only the properly end-marked managed block is replaced with the current version.
- [x] AC-6: Init honors optional target and scope input — given `target` and `scope`, the generated index records the target as a planned Target Map row (`| v2.0.0 | release | planned | Initial target |`) with Active Target still empty, lists the scope in the scope-map table, and creates the corresponding scope SRS document, numbered by the FR-NODE-088 allocation (`docs/spec/01.payments.srs.md` in an empty project), carrying the scope code (`| Scope | PAY |`).

### FR-NODE-052 — Claude settings.json key-preserving JSON merger

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL merge the SpecKiwi PostToolUse trace-hook entry into an existing `.claude/settings.json` using JSONC-aware parsing that preserves every unrelated top-level key (e.g. `$schema`, `env`, `permissions`) and every pre-existing hook. The merged `PostToolUse` entry SHALL match the `Edit`, `Write`, `MultiEdit`, and `NotebookEdit` tools and invoke the SpecKiwi trace hook (`docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs`). On write, the merger SHALL preserve the existing file's byte-order-mark (BOM) state, write UTF-8 without a BOM when creating a new file, and take a backup (`.bak`) of any prior file. A sentinel marker SHALL make re-running idempotent, so a second merge neither duplicates the entry nor otherwise mutates the file.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Merging into an existing .claude/settings.json preserves all unrelated top-level keys (such as $schema, env, and permissions) and any pre-existing hooks, adding only the SpecKiwi trace hook.
- [ ] AC-2: The merged PostToolUse hook entry matches the Edit, Write, MultiEdit, and NotebookEdit tools and points at the SpecKiwi trace hook (docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs).
- [ ] AC-3: The merger preserves the existing file's BOM state, writes UTF-8 without a BOM for a newly created file, and writes a .bak backup of any prior file before overwriting it.
- [ ] AC-4: Re-running the merge is a no-op via the sentinel marker: the trace-hook entry is not duplicated and the file is not otherwise changed.

### FR-NODE-053 — Codex apply_patch hooks.json installer with version gate and trust-review guidance

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

During `speckiwi init` hook installation, the system SHALL materialize a Codex `.codex/hooks.json` that registers a PostToolUse hook matching the `apply_patch` tool and delegating to the `docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs` runner, without silently overwriting an existing file. When an enterprise Codex policy (`allow_managed_hooks_only = true` in `.codex/config.toml`) is detected, the system SHALL skip writing `hooks.json` and emit a suppression warning; regardless of that policy, the system SHALL always surface a trust-review advisory that Codex runs project hooks only after the repository is trusted. The system SHALL also expose a version gate that decides whether a detected `codex --version` string is below the apply_patch hook support floor (0.20.0), so the installer can warn when the hook cannot be relied upon.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: AC-1 (hooks.json writer): `speckiwi init` writes `.codex/hooks.json` whose `hooks.PostToolUse` entry matches the `apply_patch` tool and runs `node docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs`; the created path is reported under `created`, and an already-present `.codex/hooks.json` is left unchanged and reported under `skipped` (never silently overwritten).
- [ ] AC-2: AC-2 (enterprise suppression + trust-review guidance): when `.codex/config.toml` sets `allow_managed_hooks_only = true`, the installer skips writing `.codex/hooks.json` and emits an enterprise-suppression warning naming that policy; independently of the policy outcome, the installer always emits a trust-review advisory stating Codex runs project hooks only after the repository is trusted.
- [ ] AC-3: AC-3 (apply_patch version gate): `isCodexVersionBelowApplyPatchFloor` reports whether a detected `codex --version` is below the floor `0.20.0` — a clearly-old version (0.0.1) is below and a clearly-new version (999.0.0) is not; the exact floor version is not below; a prerelease of the floor core (0.20.0-rc.1) is below while a prerelease of a newer core (0.21.0-rc.1) is not; the version is read from the leading anchored semver token so a noisy line (e.g. "0.19.0 (... build ...)") still compares its version; an unparseable or empty version is treated as below the floor so the caller warns.

### FR-NODE-054 — Mode-aware per-edit trace hook with per-session shard and fail-open semantics

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an installed PostToolUse hook script at `docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs` that Claude/Codex invoke with the hook payload on stdin. The hook SHALL read the work mode from `docs/spec/steps/state.md` and, only when Mode is `vibe` and an Active Task is set, append exactly one JSONL trace record per edit to a per-session shard `docs/.kiwi/trace/<ActiveTask>/trace.<sessionId>.jsonl`, deriving the changed file paths from the Claude `tool_input.file_path` or from the Codex `apply_patch` patch body. Any read/parse failure, missing or unparseable `state.md`, non-vibe mode, or missing task `intent.md` SHALL be a safe no-op that exits 0 and never blocks the edit (fail-open).

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: The hook appends a trace record only when state.md Mode is `vibe`; when Mode is a non-vibe value such as `sdd`, no trace shard is created and no record is written.
- [ ] AC-2: Records are written to a per-session shard file `docs/.kiwi/trace/<ActiveTask>/trace.<sessionId>.jsonl`, so two distinct sessions produce two separate shard files that each contain only their own records (no cross-writer interleave).
- [ ] AC-3: The hook extracts changed file paths from both the Claude `tool_input.file_path` (recorded verbatim) and the Codex `apply_patch` patch body (parsing `*** Add/Update/Delete File:` lines), placing them in the record `files` array.
- [ ] AC-4: A missing state.md, an unparseable/garbage state.md, or any non-vibe mode is treated as a no-op: the process exits 0 and creates no trace directory or file, so the edit is never blocked.
- [ ] AC-5: Each appended JSONL line conforms to the contract fields `ts` (ISO timestamp string), `agent`, `sessionId`, `tool`, `files`, `op`, and `intentRef` (pointing at the active task's intent.md), and additionally propagates the optional `turnId` when the payload carries one.
- [ ] AC-6: When Mode is `vibe` and an Active Task is set but the task's `intent.md` is missing, the hook is a no-op that appends no record and prints intent-recovery guidance mentioning intent to the operator.
- [ ] AC-7: AC-7 (FND-002 hardening): All trace writes remain contained within `docs/.kiwi/trace`; an Active Task or session id carrying `../` traversal segments never causes a `.jsonl` file to be written outside the trace tree (traversal attempt is a fail-open no-op).
- [ ] AC-8: AC-8 (FND-004 hardening): Mode-line parsing matches the core parser — when state.md contains multiple `Mode:` lines, the first Mode line wins (e.g. `vibe` then `sdd` is read as vibe).

### FR-NODE-055 — Pre-commit best-effort gate for unsynthesized vibe trace

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an installed pre-commit gate script at docs/.kiwi/hooks/pre-commit.mjs that .git/hooks/pre-commit invokes (as `node "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/docs/.kiwi/hooks/pre-commit.mjs"`) against the repository root. When the work mode recorded in docs/spec/steps/state.md is `vibe` and an Active Task is set but its per-task step SRS directory docs/spec/steps/<ActiveTask>/ does not yet exist (unsynthesized), the script SHALL block the commit by exiting non-zero and printing guidance to run the synthesis skill. In every other situation — the active task already synthesized, a non-vibe mode, no active task, or a missing/unparseable state.md — it SHALL exit zero. The gate is a documented best-effort local check: it fails open (exit zero) on any unexpected read/parse failure and can be bypassed with `git commit --no-verify`.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: When docs/spec/steps/state.md has Mode `vibe` and an Active Task set, and no matching docs/spec/steps/<ActiveTask>/ directory exists, the hook exits non-zero (blocks the commit).
- [ ] AC-2: The hook exits zero whenever the gate does not apply — specifically once the matching docs/spec/steps/<ActiveTask>/ directory exists (task synthesized), or when Mode is not `vibe` (e.g. `sdd`) even without a step directory, or when no Active Task is set.
- [ ] AC-3: On a block the hook prints guidance directing the user to run the synthesis skill (block output references synthesis), distinguishing an intentional gate block from an unrelated crash.
- [x] AC-4: The pre-commit hook installer tolerates a git worktree or submodule where .git is a `gitdir:` pointer file (not a directory): speckiwi init does not crash with ENOTDIR, detects the non-directory .git and skips the hook install with a worktree/submodule warning, and still completes the rest of init.

### FR-NODE-056 — Vibe-to-SRS step synthesis engine with idempotency, secret redaction, and diff cap

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a synthesis engine (`synthesizeStepSrs`) that merges a task's `intent.md`, its per-session trace shards, the task-name step code comments (`@step <TaskName>:`), and the final git diff into a step SRS document at `docs/spec/steps/<TaskName>/<TaskName>.srs.md`. The synthesis SHALL be idempotent: once the step SRS exists, a subsequent run SHALL be a no-op that leaves the file byte-identical. Before writing, the engine SHALL exclude gitignored paths across every input channel, cap the embedded diff section to a bounded byte budget with an explicit truncation notice, redact recognized secret patterns from all channels, and merge trace shards in ascending timestamp order while tolerating (discarding only) a torn trailing JSONL line. The returned result SHALL report `written`, `skipped`, `diffCapped`, `redactions`, and `traceEntries`.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given intent.md, one or more trace shards, source files bearing `@step <TaskName>:` comments, and diff.patch under docs/spec/steps/<TaskName>/, synthesis writes docs/spec/steps/<TaskName>/<TaskName>.srs.md and returns ok:true with { written:true, skipped:false }; the written SRS contains content drawn from each of the four input channels (intent body text, trace-shard note, step-comment text, and a diff line).
- [ ] AC-2: When the step SRS file already exists, a subsequent synthesis is an idempotent no-op that returns { written:false, skipped:true } and leaves the existing file byte-identical, even when a source input (e.g. intent.md) was mutated after the first run.
- [ ] AC-3: An oversized diff is truncated so the written SRS stays under the 64 KiB diff budget, the result reports diffCapped:true, the dropped tail content is absent, and a truncation marker (matching /truncat|capped|.../i) is present; separately, paths excluded by .gitignore — via literal directory prefixes (e.g. `secret-build/`) and trailing-glob entries (e.g. `*.env`), applied to diff `diff --git` hunk headers, trace-shard `path` fields, and step-comment source files — never surface in the SRS (neither the ignored path string nor its file contents), while non-ignored diff hunks on both sides of a dropped one survive.
- [ ] AC-4: Recognized secret patterns are replaced by a `[REDACTED]` marker across the intent, trace, comment, and diff channels so no secret survives verbatim, and the result reports the redaction count; covered classes include AWS access key id (AKIA...), GitHub classic (ghp_) and fine-grained (github_pat_) PATs, Bearer tokens, full PEM PRIVATE KEY blocks (BEGIN/END envelope plus base64 body, case-insensitive incl. lowercase) and bare header/footer lines, JWT, Slack (xox...), OpenAI-style (sk-...) keys, and plausible key=value / key: value credential assignments; legitimate code/prose that merely contains a key word (function-call values, `===` comparisons, dotted member access, prose after `key:`) is NOT redacted (redactions:0 for such inputs), while genuine credentials (no-space assignment, colon+space token with a digit, quoted value) are still redacted.
- [ ] AC-5: Trace shards are merged in ascending `ts` timestamp order regardless of shard filename order; a partially-written (unparseable) trailing JSONL line is discarded while every well-formed entry is retained; synthesis still succeeds with written:true, and the result's traceEntries equals the count of surviving well-formed (and non-gitignored) entries.

### FR-NODE-057 — Reservation-minted step requirement IDs with unreserved-ID promotion guard

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL mint step requirement IDs during vibe synthesis only through a tool-governed reservation minter (mintStepRequirementId) that returns a deterministic next-slot Requirement ID computed over the combined view of HEAD, step, and reserved IDs, never by free-text selection. The system SHALL NOT allow a synthesis step to assign a literal Requirement ID of its own choosing; the minted ID is derived purely from the supplied view. When promoting a step requirement into a body scope, the system SHALL reject any Requirement ID that the reservation minter would not have produced, and SHALL leave the target body scope document unchanged in that case.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a reservation view whose HEAD, step, and reserved sets each occupy a distinct slot (e.g. headIds=[FR-ARCH-001], stepIds=[FR-ARCH-005], reservedIds=[FR-ARCH-002]), mintStepRequirementId returns the next slot above the maximum occupied slot across the union of all three sets (FR-ARCH-006) and returns an ID that collides with none of the three view sets.
- [ ] AC-2: The minted ID is a deterministic function of the view alone: over headIds=[FR-ARCH-001] with empty step and reserved sets the minter returns FR-ARCH-002, and calling the minter again with the just-minted ID added to reservedIds advances to FR-ARCH-003 — the minter is the single source of the ID and a synthesis step cannot substitute a caller-chosen free-text literal.
- [ ] AC-3: promote_step_requirement denies promotion of a Requirement ID the reservation minter would never produce (a free-text id such as FR-ARCH-501 when the minter over the view would yield FR-ARCH-002): the result reports ok=false with a non-empty error message, and the target body scope document (10.product-architecture.srs.md) is left byte-for-byte unchanged, with neither the unreserved id nor any minter-shaped id inserted.

### FR-NODE-058 — Vibe merge contradiction completion hard-gate distinguishing synthesized from contradiction-verified

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL evaluate a vibe completion gate that prevents a vibe synthesis/merge step from being marked complete while its touched dirty-edge closure contains unacknowledged contradictions. An edge SHALL be treated as a contradiction unless its classification is "clean", and completion SHALL be permitted only when the closure is contradiction-verified (contains no contradiction) or the remaining contradictions are explicitly acknowledged. Being SYNTHESIZED (a step directory exists) SHALL be orthogonal to being contradiction-verified and SHALL NOT, by itself, grant completion. For non-vibe STEP-namespace flows the gate SHALL remain advisory-only: it SHALL never be enforced and SHALL never block completion.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: For a vibe flow (vibe=true) whose dirty-edge closure is non-empty (at least one non-clean edge) and unacknowledged, the gate returns allowed=false, blockedReason="dirty-edges-unacknowledged", and enforced=true (the vibe hard-gate fires).
- [ ] AC-2: Step-directory existence (stepDirectoryExists=true) alone does not satisfy the contradiction-verified condition: a synthesized vibe step with remaining unacknowledged contradictions is still blocked (allowed=false), while the same synthesized state with an empty closure is allowed (allowed=true) — completion is granted by the empty closure, not the step directory.
- [ ] AC-3: For a vibe flow, explicit acknowledgement (acknowledged=true) of remaining dirty edges allows completion (allowed=true) with blockedReason absent.
- [ ] AC-4: For a non-vibe flow (vibe=false), the identical non-empty, unacknowledged dirty closure does not block completion (allowed=true) and the gate is not enforced (enforced=false); non-vibe STEP-namespace diagnostics remain advisory.

### FR-NODE-059 — Retarget Core Mutation with Per-Item Dry-Run Plan and Requirement-Scoped Schema

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a core `retarget` mutation that reassigns the `Target` metadata of a caller-supplied set of requirement ids to a single destination target through a per-item loop. The mutation SHALL default to dry-run, returning a per-item plan in which each id resolves to either a planned `Target` rewrite or a skip reason, and SHALL write `Target` metadata changes to disk only when dry-run is explicitly disabled. The mutation input contract SHALL structurally exclude any status field and any active-target field, so the mutation can neither finalize requirements nor change the Active Target. Destination-target registration, frozen-block change control, and an explicit exclude list SHALL each be enforced per item, and writes SHALL preserve each file's existing newline style.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: The retarget input type structurally excludes a `status` key and both an `activeTarget` and an `active-target` key, while still exposing the destination `toTarget` and the requirement id set `ids: string[]`; a caller therefore cannot finalize requirements or change the Active Target through this contract.
- [ ] AC-2: When no dry-run flag is supplied, retarget defaults to dry-run (`dryRun === true`) and returns a per-item plan; the entry for each requirement id carries either a planned Target rewrite (`fromTarget` and `toTarget`, e.g. v1.0.0 -> v1.1.0) or a `skipReason`, and no spec file is modified.
- [ ] AC-3: A requirement whose destination target is not present in the index Target Map (docs/spec/00.index.md) receives `skipReason` = 'target-not-registered' with no planned `toTarget`, and its Target row is left unchanged even when dry-run is disabled.
- [ ] AC-4: A frozen requirement (Stability=frozen) is skipped with `skipReason` = 'frozen-needs-change-note' when no reason is supplied and its Target is not rewritten; when a reason is supplied the rewrite proceeds (no skipReason) and the reason is appended to that requirement's Change Notes.
- [ ] AC-5: A verified requirement (Status=verified) is included in the rewrite plan by default (planned `toTarget`, no skipReason); an explicit `exclude` list removes only the listed ids, which receive `skipReason` = 'excluded' with no planned `toTarget`.
- [ ] AC-6: Only when dry-run is disabled does retarget write the Target metadata rewrite to disk (the Target row changes from the old to the new value), and the write preserves the file's existing newline style — an LF file stays LF and a CRLF file stays CRLF.

### FR-NODE-060 — update-field core mutation with ID-prefix migration for type and scope edits

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an `update-field` core mutation that edits a single metadata field of one requirement identified by id. For line-replacement fields (priority, risk, title, target, verification-method) it SHALL rewrite exactly the one targeted line and leave every other line of the requirement and file unchanged. For migration fields (type, scope) it SHALL regenerate the requirement id so its prefix matches the new type/scope — allocating the first free slot via `generateNextRequirementId` — default to a non-writing dry-run that reports the old id, the generated new id, and the planned block move, and require an explicit sign-off before writing; a confirmed migration SHALL also rewrite inbound Trace Links Requirement references from the old id to the new id. An unknown requirement id SHALL return `ok=false` with `error.code=NOT_FOUND` and write nothing.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Calling update-field with a line-replacement field (e.g. field=priority, one of priority/risk/title/target/verification-method) returns ok=true and rewrites only that single metadata line; the file keeps the same total line count and exactly one line differs (the targeted row, e.g. '| Priority | low |' replacing '| Priority | high |').
- [ ] AC-2: Calling update-field with field=type on a confirmed write (signOff=true, dryRun=false) regenerates the requirement id so the prefix matches the new type/scope, allocated as the first free slot via generateNextRequirementId (functional/ARCH FR-ARCH-001 migrating to non_functional yields NFR-ARCH-001); the written spec contains the new heading and the updated '| Type | non_functional |' row and no longer contains the old heading, and the output reports oldId and newId.
- [ ] AC-3: Calling update-field on a migration field (type/scope) with neither dryRun nor signOff supplied defaults to dry-run: the output has dryRun=true, oldId, the generated newId, and a defined plannedMove, and no file is written (the spec is byte-for-byte identical to before).
- [ ] AC-4: Calling update-field on a migration field with dryRun=false but no signOff returns ok=false and writes nothing (the old heading is preserved); with signOff=true and dryRun=false the migration is applied and inbound Trace Links rows of Type Requirement referencing the old id are rewritten to the new id (e.g. '| Requirement | NFR-ARCH-001 | depends_on | - |' replaces '| Requirement | FR-ARCH-001 | depends_on | - |').
- [ ] AC-5: Calling update-field with an unknown requirement id returns ok=false with error.code=NOT_FOUND and writes no file changes.

### FR-NODE-061 — add-related-doc and add-change-note core mutations for structural metadata

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide two core mutation functions that edit a requirement's structural metadata in place. `addRelatedDoc` SHALL append a single document reference to a requirement's `Related Docs` metadata value by rewriting only that one metadata line, and `addChangeNote` SHALL append a single dated row to a requirement's `Change Notes` table while preserving all existing rows. Both mutations SHALL reject any cell value containing a pipe or newline with a MUTATION_DENIED result and no write, return NOT_FOUND for an unknown requirement id, support a non-writing dry-run that returns a patch summary, and surface STALE_PATCH when the target file changes before the write is applied.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: addRelatedDoc appends one document reference to the requirement's Related Docs metadata value and rewrites only that single metadata line — a bare '-' placeholder is replaced by the reference and an existing comma-separated value has the reference appended — leaving every other section byte-for-byte unchanged, and returns a mutation-ok value carrying { id, reference, written }.
- [ ] AC-2: addChangeNote appends exactly one dated row with date, change, and reason cells to the requirement's Change Notes table, preserving all pre-existing rows and leaving unrelated sections unchanged, and returns a mutation-ok value carrying { id, written }.
- [ ] AC-3: Any Related Docs reference (addRelatedDoc) or any Change Notes date/change/reason cell (addChangeNote) that contains a pipe or newline character is rejected with a MUTATION_DENIED result and no file is written.
- [ ] AC-4: A request targeting a requirement id that does not exist returns a NOT_FOUND result and performs no write.
- [ ] AC-5: When dryRun is set, both mutations return a patch summary (single operation, dryRun=true, containing the previewed replacement line or row) and write nothing to disk.

### FR-NODE-062 — Restore mutation un-discards a requirement with a required reason

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a core `restore` mutation that un-discards a previously discarded requirement through the same hardened patch path as `update_status`. When invoked with a non-empty reason, the mutation SHALL set the requirement's Status to the requested active status (defaulting to `planned`), remove the discarded heading decoration (strikethrough and the `[DISCARDED]` marker), and append exactly one Change Notes row carrying the supplied reason within the same atomic patch. A reason SHALL be mandatory: when it is missing or empty the mutation SHALL fail (ok=false) and leave the document byte-identical. When the requirement being restored still carries checked acceptance criteria or verification evidence retained from before it was discarded, the mutation SHALL still succeed but SHALL surface an advisory warning that this AC/evidence may be stale.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Restoring a discarded requirement with no explicit status sets its Status to `planned` (and clears `discarded`); supplying an explicit active status such as `in_progress` sets Status to exactly that value.
- [ ] AC-2: Restore removes the heading strikethrough (`~~...~~`) and the `[DISCARDED]` marker, leaving a clean requirement heading of the form `### <ID> — <title>`.
- [ ] AC-3: Restore appends exactly one Change Notes row (one net new dated row) that contains the supplied reason text.
- [ ] AC-4: Restore invoked with no reason, or with an empty-string reason, returns ok=false and writes no file — the spec document is unchanged.
- [ ] AC-5: Restoring a requirement that was previously verified (still holding checked acceptance criteria and/or verification evidence) returns ok=true and emits at least one warning — surfaced either on the result value's `warnings` array or in the top-level diagnostics as severity `warning` — whose text references stale acceptance criteria / evidence.

### FR-NODE-063 — Set supersede metadata with paired trace link

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |

| Priority | medium |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a core setSupersede mutation that writes a requirement's Supersedes and/or Superseded By metadata field into its "| Field | Value |" table without disturbing any other metadata line. When trace synchronization is requested, the same call SHALL also insert a matching Trace Link row (relation `supersedes` or `superseded_by`) in a single patch. The mutation SHALL support dry-run preview and SHALL fail without writing when the target requirement id does not exist.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a requirement whose metadata table has no Supersedes row, calling setSupersede with a supersedes value adds exactly one `| Supersedes | <value> |` metadata row, writes no Superseded By row, leaves every pre-existing metadata line unchanged (row count increases by exactly one), and returns ok=true with { id, written: true }.
- [ ] AC-2: Calling setSupersede with a supersededBy value adds exactly one `| Superseded By | <value> |` metadata row, writes no Supersedes row, leaves all other metadata lines unchanged, and returns ok=true with { id, written: true }.
- [ ] AC-3: When trace sync is enabled, the call additionally inserts a Trace Link row of Type=Requirement referencing the given value with relation `supersedes` for a supersedes value (or relation `superseded_by` for a supersededBy value), in addition to the corresponding metadata row.
- [ ] AC-4: An unknown requirement id returns ok=false with error code NOT_FOUND and leaves the spec file byte-for-byte unchanged.
- [ ] AC-5: A dry-run call returns ok=true with { id, written: false } plus a patch preview (patch.dryRun=true, a non-empty preview array, and filePath resolving to the requirement's spec file), and leaves the on-disk file byte-for-byte unchanged.

### FR-NODE-064 — Register unregistered scope documents into the index Scope Map

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a registerScopes core mutation that detects scope documents (docs/spec/*.srs.md) present on disk but absent from the index Scope Map — the SRS-W018 drift set — and registers each one by appending a single Scope Map row to 00.index.md. The row's prefix SHALL be inferred from the requirement id prefixes within the document (the middle token of a requirement id, e.g. FR-EXTRA-001 yields EXTRA). The mutation SHALL default to dry-run, previewing the documents and inferred prefixes it would register while writing no file, and SHALL apply changes only when explicitly requested. It SHALL skip any document whose inferred prefix collides with an already-registered prefix and SHALL never modify a Requirement Block or the index Status / Requirement Type summary counts.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: When invoked with no apply flag (the default), registerScopes runs in dry-run mode (dryRun=true), returns a per-item plan naming each unregistered scope document with its prefix inferred from the document's requirement id prefixes (e.g. EXTRA for a document holding FR-EXTRA-001), and leaves the index file byte-identical to before with no reference to the unregistered document added.
- [ ] AC-2: When invoked with apply=true, registerScopes runs with dryRun=false and inserts exactly one Scope Map row per unregistered scope document, so that after apply the index Scope Map names each newly registered document exactly once together with its inferred prefix.
- [ ] AC-3: An unregistered scope document whose inferred prefix collides with a prefix already present in the Scope Map is skipped with skipReason 'prefix-conflict' and is not written as a Scope Map row, while any non-colliding unregistered document processed in the same run is still registered.
- [ ] AC-4: After apply, registerScopes leaves every Requirement Block in the scope documents byte-identical and leaves the index Status Summary and Requirement Type Summary count tables byte-identical to before — it only inserts Scope Map rows.

### FR-NODE-065 — Scaffold and register a new scope

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a scaffoldScope core mutation that creates a brand-new numbered scope srs.md document from the scope template and registers it in the index in a single operation. Unlike registerScopes (FR-NODE-053), which only adds Scope Map rows for already-existing unregistered documents, scaffoldScope both CREATES the document and registers it, adding exactly one row to the index §2 SRS Documents section and one row to the §4 Scope Map section. The mutation SHALL default to dry-run when no apply flag is supplied, returning a preview of the new file body and both index rows while writing nothing. It SHALL reject a scope whose name or prefix collides with an already-registered scope, returning an unsuccessful result and writing no file.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a name and prefix with apply=true, scaffoldScope creates a new document whose file name has a leading numeric prefix and the .srs.md suffix (distinct from any existing document, e.g. not the already-registered 10.product-architecture.srs.md); the created file exists on disk and its body contains the scope template frontmatter carrying the chosen prefix (| Document Type | scope_srs | and | Scope | <PREFIX> |) and the template sections (## 1. Scope Overview, ## 4. Requirements).
- [ ] AC-2: With apply=true, exactly one row is added to the §2 SRS Documents section and exactly one row to the §4 Scope Map section (each section's row count increases by one and no pre-existing row is altered); each new row references the created document's file name and its prefix.

### FR-NODE-066 — Kiwi skill install shared-resource mirror sync

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | skills, install, shared, mirror |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi's Kiwi skill installation service must materialize the `_shared/kiwi/*` resources that installed skills reference into the destination root `_shared/kiwi/` mirror, keeping the mirror equal to the union of `../_shared/kiwi/*` references across all skills present in the destination root, and pruning mirror shared files that no installed skill references.

#### Rationale

The install service validates and records shared `_shared/kiwi/*` references in metadata but never copies those files into the install mirror, so a fresh install produces skills whose `../_shared/kiwi/*` links resolve to files that do not exist in the destination — broken on arrival. `SKILL_NAME_PATTERN` also rejects `_shared`, so the shared resources cannot be mirrored through the normal skill-copy path. The installer must own reference-based materialization of the shared mirror. Prune must use the union across all destination skills, not just the current invocation, so reinstalling a single skill never deletes shared files that other installed skills still reference.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: AC-1: After a non-dry-run install, every `_shared/kiwi/*` file referenced by an installed skill is copied from the source shared root into the destination root `_shared/kiwi/` mirror with byte-identical content and preserved file mode.
- [ ] AC-2: AC-2: The desired shared mirror set is the union of `../_shared/kiwi/*` references across all skill directories present in the destination root, not only the skills in the current invocation, so a single-skill reinstall does not remove shared files still referenced by other installed skills.
- [ ] AC-3: AC-3: Shared files present in the destination `_shared/kiwi/` mirror that are referenced by no installed skill are pruned, leaving the mirror equal to the reference union.
- [ ] AC-4: AC-4: Existing skill-copy behavior, `SKILL.md` normalization, install metadata (`sharedResourceReferences`, `sharedResourceRoot`), safe-path/symlink rejection, and the mandatory MCP token contract are unchanged, and `_shared` is never installed as a skill directory.
- [ ] AC-5: AC-5: An automated test installs skills with shared references and asserts mirror parity: referenced shared files present and content-matched, stale mirror shared files pruned, and cross-skill shared files preserved on single-skill reinstall.

#### Verification Evidence

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- Shared mirror lives at destinationRoot/_shared/kiwi (sibling of installed skill dirs), matching the existing metadata sharedResourceRoot. Compute the desired set by scanning every skill directory under destinationRoot for shared references (union across all installed skills), copy referenced files from sourceRoot/_shared/kiwi, then prune mirror files not in the union. Run once per install invocation after all skill packages are placed; skip entirely when the reference union is empty to avoid touching unrelated content.

### FR-NODE-067 — speckiwi init MCP server project registration (.mcp.json idempotent merge)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | init-onboarding, mcp-registration |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The core `initProject` service gains an MCP registration step (invoked by CLI init, controllable via options) that registers the SpecKiwi stdio MCP server into the project's Claude Code `.mcp.json`. It reads an existing `.mcp.json` (or creates a new one) and inserts an `mcpServers.speckiwi` entry (command + args to launch the stdio server) ONLY if that key is absent, merging non-destructively so all other `mcpServers` entries and top-level keys are preserved, and writing atomically (temp file + rename). If a `speckiwi` server key already exists, the step performs no write and reports the registration under `skipped` (idempotent). If `.mcp.json` exists but is not parseable as JSON, the step refuses to write and emits a warning with manual-setup instructions (no clobber, no data loss). Codex MCP registration is NOT performed by editing the global `~/.codex/config.toml`; instead the step emits a warning containing the exact command/snippet the user can run to register speckiwi for Codex. In dry-run mode the intended action (create/skip) is reported without any filesystem write.

#### Rationale

Onboarding friction: users must hand-configure the SpecKiwi MCP server today (no registration code exists). A 5-lens decision committee chose direct config-file registration with idempotent detect->skip (4:1), with the risk lens's hard constraint that the global ~/.codex/config.toml must NOT be hand-edited (no TOML parser present; corruption of a global cross-project file is unrecoverable).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a project with no `.mcp.json`, when init runs with MCP registration enabled, then `.mcp.json` is created containing `mcpServers.speckiwi` with a command and args that launch the stdio MCP server, and the action is reported under created.
- [x] AC-2: Given a `.mcp.json` that already contains a `speckiwi` server key, when init runs, then the file content is left unchanged and the registration is reported under skipped.
- [x] AC-3: Given a `.mcp.json` containing other `mcpServers` entries and top-level keys but no `speckiwi`, when init runs, then the `speckiwi` key is added and every pre-existing entry/key is preserved (non-destructive merge), written atomically.
- [x] AC-4: Given a `.mcp.json` that is not valid JSON, when init runs, then the file is not overwritten and a warning with manual-setup instructions is emitted.
- [x] AC-5: Codex MCP registration does not write `~/.codex/config.toml`; instead init emits a warning containing the exact command/snippet to register speckiwi for Codex.
- [x] AC-6: In dry-run mode the MCP registration action is reported in the plan (create or skip) with zero writes to `.mcp.json`.
