### FR-NODE-068 — speckiwi init project skill provisioning (Claude + Codex, reuse installer)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | init-onboarding, skill-install |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The core `initProject` service gains a skill-provisioning step (invoked by CLI init, controllable via options) that installs the bundled kiwi skills into the project for the fixed Claude and Codex agent pair — claude into `.claude/skills`, codex into `.agents/skills` — at project scope, reusing the existing skill installer's install/update/skip/conflict classification and atomic staged replace. Per-skill results are folded into init's `created`/`updated`/`skipped`/`warnings` report. A conflict (foreign or mismatched install metadata at a destination) is reported as a warning and does not abort init. If a bundled skills source root cannot be located, init emits a warning and completes its remaining steps (the skills step degrades non-fatally). By default, provisioning targets the project destinations; the global `~/.claude/skills` is used only under the `--global` option (see FR-NODE-084). In dry-run mode a per-skill plan is produced with zero writes.

#### Rationale

The skill installer exists as a separate command; the user asked init to also install project skills. A 5-lens committee chose a fixed Claude+Codex project-scope install (4:1), matching init's existing dual AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md + dual-hook policy, reusing the existing atomic installer rather than a new code path.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a fresh project, when init runs with skills enabled, then the bundled kiwi skills are installed under `.claude/skills` and `.agents/skills` and each install is reported under created or updated.
- [x] AC-2: Re-running init with unchanged skill sources leaves the installed skills byte-identical and reports them under skipped (idempotent).
- [x] AC-3: A skill destination that conflicts (foreign or mismatched install metadata) is reported as a warning and does not abort the remaining init steps.
- [x] AC-4: If the bundled skills source root cannot be located, init emits a warning and still completes its remaining steps (non-fatal skills degrade).
- [x] AC-5: By default (without the global option), skill provisioning uses project scope: destinations are the project `.claude/skills` and `.agents/skills`. The global home skills directory is used only when the global option is enabled (see FR-NODE-084).
- [x] AC-6: In dry-run mode the per-skill planned operations are reported with zero filesystem writes.

### FR-NODE-069 — speckiwi init orphaned kiwi-* skill prune (metadata-gated, drift-safe)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | init-onboarding, skill-install, orphan-prune |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

As part of init skill provisioning, `initProject` removes orphaned `kiwi-*` skill directories from the project skill destinations. A directory is eligible for removal ONLY if all of the following hold: (a) it is a direct child of the destination skills root whose name starts with `kiwi-`; (b) it carries SpecKiwi install metadata (`.speckiwi-skill-install.json`) identifying it as a speckiwi-managed runtime mirror for the matching agent; (c) it is ABSENT from the newly resolved bundled source skill set (a true orphan of a renamed or removed skill); (d) it is not a symlink and contains no symlinked entries; and (e) its on-disk contents match the recorded install checksum. On checksum drift (uncommitted user edits) the directory is NOT deleted and a warning is emitted instead. A `kiwi-*` directory lacking speckiwi install metadata (user-authored) is never removed, and non-`kiwi-*` directories are never touched. Removed directories are listed in a `removed` output array. The prune runs only after the bundled source set is successfully resolved, so a source-resolution error can never leave the destination with deleted-and-not-reinstalled skills. In dry-run mode the directories that would be removed are reported without any deletion.

#### Rationale

The user explicitly asked init to remove previous-version skills (those starting with kiwi-) before installing. The per-skill installer can never remove renamed/deleted skills (e.g. the just-removed vibe/merge group). All 5 committee lenses voted YES to an orphan prune, unanimously conditioned on strict metadata/symlink/drift guardrails so a user-authored kiwi-named skill or local edits are never destroyed.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a project skill destination containing a speckiwi-managed `kiwi-*` directory that is absent from the current source set (an orphan of a removed skill), when init runs, then that directory is removed and listed in `removed`.
- [x] AC-2: A `kiwi-*` directory without `.speckiwi-skill-install.json` (user-authored) is never removed, even though its name matches `kiwi-*`.
- [x] AC-3: A non-`kiwi-*` skill directory is never removed by the prune, regardless of metadata.
- [x] AC-4: A speckiwi-managed `kiwi-*` directory that IS present in the current source set is not pruned (it is updated or skipped by provisioning, not deleted).
- [x] AC-5: Given a speckiwi-managed `kiwi-*` orphan whose on-disk contents drifted from the recorded checksum, when init runs, then it is NOT deleted and a warning is emitted.
- [x] AC-6: The prune never follows symlinks: a symlinked candidate is refused with a warning rather than removed.
- [x] AC-7: In dry-run mode the orphan directories that would be removed are reported in the plan with zero deletions.

### FR-NODE-070 — speckiwi init unified dry-run preview and created/updated/skipped/removed/warnings envelope

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | init-onboarding, dry-run, idempotency |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The core `initProject` service supports a dry-run mode that computes and returns the full plan of all init steps — SRS scaffold, AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md upsert, governance hooks, MCP registration, skill provisioning, and orphaned kiwi-* prune — without performing any filesystem write or deletion. The init result envelope exposes `created`, `updated`, `skipped`, `warnings`, and a new `removed` array so every mutating step (including the prune) is reported. All new mutating steps (MCP registration, skill provisioning, prune) execute inside the existing SRS mutation lock envelope with no unlocked writes. Re-running init on an already-initialized project produces zero churn for the new steps: MCP registration is skipped, unchanged skills are skipped, and the prune yields an empty `removed` array. Dry-run output is structurally identical (same fields) to a real run, differing only in that nothing is written.

#### Rationale

init currently has no dry-run in InitProjectInput, yet the new steps include destructive prune and config writes. Governance, risk, and user-value lenses all require a unified dry-run preview and a `removed` array so every mutating step is reported, and second-run idempotency (zero churn) is provable.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: In dry-run mode `initProject` performs zero filesystem writes and deletions and returns a plan covering scaffold, agent files, hooks, MCP registration, skill provisioning, and prune.
- [x] AC-2: The init result envelope includes a `removed` array in addition to created/updated/skipped/warnings, populated by the orphan prune.
- [x] AC-3: All new mutating steps (MCP registration, skill provisioning, prune) execute within the existing SRS mutation lock envelope.
- [x] AC-4: A second init run on an already-initialized project yields empty created/updated/removed for the new steps (MCP skipped, skills skipped, no orphans).
- [x] AC-5: Dry-run output has the same envelope fields as a real run, differing only in that no filesystem change occurred.

#### Requirement

The work-mode model over docs/spec/steps/state.md SHALL support mode `tdd`: getWorkMode SHALL return mode "tdd" together with its Active Task when state.md declares them, and setWorkMode SHALL accept mode "tdd", SHALL persist or rewrite the Active Task line for tdd exactly as it does for vibe, and SHALL keep dropping a stale Active Task line when switching to sdd or wait. Existing sdd/vibe/wait behavior, cell-injection guards, and the fail-open read contract stay unchanged.

#### Rationale

TDD First mode stores its on/off state in the existing work-mode metadata block (committee decision Q1:A); the model layer must treat tdd with vibe-parity Active Task semantics (G1).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN state.md declares `Mode: tdd` and `Active Task: <name>` THE getWorkMode call SHALL return {mode:"tdd", activeTask:<name>}.
- [x] AC-2: WHEN setWorkMode is called with {mode:"tdd", activeTask} THE mutation SHALL persist both the Mode and Active Task lines in the metadata block (insert or in-place rewrite).
- [x] AC-3: WHEN setWorkMode switches from tdd to sdd or wait THE mutation SHALL drop the stale Active Task line (FND-001 parity).
- [x] AC-4: WHEN existing sdd/vibe/wait flows run THE behavior SHALL be unchanged (regression).

### FR-NODE-072 — completion hard-gate enforces tdd flows

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | tdd-mode |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The step completion hard-gate (FR-NODE-058 evaluateVibeCompletionGate) SHALL be generalized so tdd flows are enforced identically to vibe flows: a tdd completion flow with unacknowledged dirty (contradiction) edges SHALL be blocked with enforced:true, an acknowledged or clean tdd flow SHALL pass with enforced:true, and flows that are neither vibe nor tdd SHALL remain advisory-only (allowed:true, enforced:false) exactly as today.

#### Rationale

Research finding F1 (docs/research/tddmode/04): internal.ts:172 hard-codes enforcement to the vibe flag, so tdd completion would be advisory no-op without this generalization — the core half of G9 in the atomic G1+G9 rollout.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN a tdd flow evaluates the gate with an unacknowledged non-clean edge THE result SHALL be {allowed:false, blockedReason:"dirty-edges-unacknowledged", enforced:true}.
- [x] AC-2: WHEN a tdd flow evaluates the gate with acknowledged or clean edges THE result SHALL be {allowed:true, enforced:true}.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN a non-vibe non-tdd flow evaluates the gate THE result SHALL remain {allowed:true, enforced:false} (advisory, regression).
- [x] AC-4: WHEN a vibe flow evaluates the gate THE existing FR-NODE-058 behavior SHALL be unchanged (regression).

#### Requirement

promoteStepRequirement SHALL read the persisted work-mode and, when it is tdd, SHALL refuse with error code EVIDENCE_REQUIRED (no write) to promote a step requirement block whose Verification Evidence section carries no evidence entry. When the work-mode is not tdd, a missing-evidence promote SHALL proceed as today but SHALL surface an advisory warning naming the promoted requirement id. Blocks carrying at least one evidence entry promote unchanged in every mode.

G4 (docs/research/tddmode/00 §8, 02 주제4): a post-hoc SRS whose requirements carry no evidence links reproduces the implementation-bias/traceability-loss failure mode; the tdd flow makes evidence density&gt;0 a hard completion gate while non-tdd flows keep backward compatibility with an advisory (enforced-for-mode pattern of FR-NODE-058).

- [x] AC-2: WHEN work-mode is tdd and the block has at least one evidence entry THE promote SHALL succeed exactly as today.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN work-mode is not tdd and the block has zero evidence entries THE promote SHALL succeed and the result SHALL carry an advisory missing-evidence warning.
- [x] AC-4: WHEN existing promote guards run (canonical id verbatim, global uniqueness, non-empty markdown, scope resolution) THE behavior SHALL be unchanged (regression).

#### Requirement

renderAgentInstructionSnippet SHALL bump AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION to "1.5" and the snippet body SHALL additionally document: (a) checking the persisted work-mode before starting work (speckiwi mode CLI); (b) the tdd cycle for step-scoped work — SDS design.md per SDS-MD rules with EARS acceptance contracts, then failing tests translated from the SDS-ACs, then implementation to green, regression, synthesis, and promote with evidence; (c) the three tdd gates — no tests without an SDS, tests are committed first and never weakened to reach green, no promote without verification evidence; (d) that in tdd mode the SRS-first rule is replaced for step-scoped work by the SDS-and-red-test-first obligation plus the mandatory post-hoc promote, while body-scope work keeps the existing rules; and (e) that edits to existing body requirements and large architecture changes stay in sdd mode. The existing versioned-marker upsert SHALL replace a v1.4 block with exactly one v1.5 block on re-init.

#### Rationale

G6 (docs/research/tddmode/00 §6, F3): the CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md hard rule "no implementation without an SRS requirement" must be atomically superseded for tdd step-scoped work when the mode ships, or agents will either refuse tdd work or implement without discipline.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN the snippet renders THE heading SHALL read `# SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 v1.5` and AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION SHALL equal "1.5".
- [x] AC-2: WHEN the snippet renders THE body SHALL contain the work-mode check instruction, the tdd cycle description, and all three tdd gates.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN the snippet renders THE body SHALL state the sdd fallback for body-scope work and the boundary rule (existing body-REQ edits and large architecture changes stay in sdd).
- [x] AC-4: WHEN init runs over a file carrying the v1.4 block THE upsert SHALL leave exactly one v1.5 block (idempotent replacement, regression of FR-NODE-051 mechanics).

### FR-NODE-076 — init installs the bundled SDS-MD rules document

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

`speckiwi init` SHALL install the bundled SDS-MD Authoring Rules document to docs/rule/SDS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md with the same mechanics as the SRS-MD rules: loaded from the packaged docs/rule copy (with a minimal ENOENT fallback), written via writeIfMissing (a pre-existing file is never overwritten without force), honoring dryRun. The npm package files whitelist SHALL include docs/rule/SDS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md so packaged installs carry the document the v1.6 agent snippet references.

#### Rationale

The v1.5 agent snippet tells agents to author the SDS per SDS-MD Authoring Rules, but init never ships that document — a freshly initialized project references rules that do not exist locally (user-reported init/CLAUDE.md gap, 2026-07-16).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN init runs on an empty repository THE docs/rule/SDS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md file SHALL be created with the bundled SDS rules content (required headings present).
- [x] AC-2: WHEN init runs again, or over a pre-existing docs/rule/SDS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md THE file SHALL be left unchanged (writeIfMissing idempotency), and WHEN dryRun is set THE file SHALL not be written.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN the npm package manifest is read THE files whitelist SHALL include docs/rule/SDS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md (packaged installs resolve the bundle).

Supersedes the v1.5 version literal in FR-NODE-075 AC-1/AC-4 (bump contract per FR-FLOW-016 AC-2).

- [x] AC-3: WHEN the snippet renders THE SDS reference SHALL name the installed path docs/rule/SDS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md, and the v1.5 tdd cycle, three gates, sdd fallback, and boundary rule SHALL remain present.

### FR-NODE-078 — merged step transition enforces the completion contradiction gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | tdd-mode, sds-tooling |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/tddmode/05.sds-tooling-gaps.md |

#### Requirement

updateStepState MUST, when transitioning a step to merged while the persisted work-mode is vibe or tdd, evaluate the completion gate (evaluateVibeCompletionGate, FR-NODE-058/FR-NODE-072) against the dirty compatibility edges restricted to the step's TouchesReq closure, refuse the transition with a stable error code when non-clean edges are unacknowledged, and allow an explicit acknowledged input to override; in other work-modes the merged transition stays unguarded.

#### Rationale

Gap G3 (docs/research/tddmode/05.sds-tooling-gaps.md): evaluateVibeCompletionGate is verified but has zero production callers, so the "merged only after contradiction verification" design is unwired. Committee kept the gate in core (promote EVIDENCE_REQUIRED precedent) with an acknowledged escape hatch to avoid hard-block regressions.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN work-mode is vibe or tdd AND a merged transition is requested AND a non-clean dirty edge involves a requirement in the step's TouchesReq closure AND acknowledged is not set THEN updateStepState fails with a stable completion-gate error code and writes nothing.
- [x] AC-2: WHEN the same blocked state is retried with acknowledged=true THEN the merged transition succeeds.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN all dirty edges are clean or involve only requirements outside the step's TouchesReq closure THEN the merged transition succeeds without acknowledgement.
- [x] AC-4: WHEN work-mode is wait or sdd THEN the merged transition succeeds without gate evaluation (enforced=false semantics preserved).
- [x] AC-5: The MCP update_step_state tool exposes the acknowledged input.

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-NODE-081 — set SDS status mutation enforces the design lifecycle state machine

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | tdd-mode, sds-tooling |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/tddmode/05.sds-tooling-gaps.md |

#### Requirement

A mutation MUST update the design.md metadata table Status cell for a step while enforcing the SDS-MD Rules v1.0.0 §6 lifecycle (forward-only transitions among draft, agreed, superseded), rejecting out-of-enum values and backward transitions with stable error codes and writing nothing on rejection, supporting dryRun, and exposed as CLI `speckiwi step sds-status <task> <status>` and MCP `set_sds_status`.

#### Rationale

Gap G8 (docs/research/tddmode/05.sds-tooling-gaps.md): the SDS Status is a closed lifecycle enum edited by hand today. Committee D4:A (4:1) — an unvalidated patch tool would automate gate bypass (worse than manual editing), so the tool must promote §6 from honor-system to enforced, mirroring guarded mutations like update_status/update_stability.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN the current Status is draft and agreed is requested THEN only the Status cell line is patched and the envelope reports the transition.
- [x] AC-2: WHEN a backward transition is requested (e.g. agreed to draft, or any transition out of superseded) THEN the mutation fails with a stable invalid-transition code and writes nothing.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN an out-of-enum status value is requested THEN the mutation fails with a stable code and writes nothing.
- [x] AC-4: WHEN the step has no design.md or the metadata Status row is missing THEN the mutation fails with a clear error instead of creating content.
- [x] AC-5: CLI and MCP surfaces are registered with FR-ARCH-006 parity, mutation-kind contract, zero-drift, and appendix tool-table suites green, and dryRun writes nothing.
- [x] AC-6: WHEN the metadata Status row carries alignment padding THEN the patched line preserves the original cell padding and replaces only the value token (no table re-normalization).

### FR-NODE-083 — doctor detects codex skills mirror drift

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | tdd-mode, sds-tooling, doctor, skills |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/tddmode/05.sds-tooling-gaps.md |

#### Requirement

doctor MUST compare the bundled codex skills source tree (packaged skills/codex) against the workspace codex install mirror (.agents/skills) and warn when a skill present in the source set is missing from or diverges in the mirror, deriving the expected skill set by scanning the source tree (no hardcoded list) and emitting a remediation pointing at `speckiwi skills install codex all`; workspaces without a .agents/skills mirror MUST NOT warn.

#### Rationale

Gap G10 (docs/research/tddmode/05.sds-tooling-gaps.md): the tracked .agents/skills mirror silently drifted from skills/codex (kiwi-step/kiwi-wave-master/kiwi-tdd missing). Committee D6:B (4:1) — recurring silent mirror drift class (loop-option _shared desync precedent) warrants a loud doctor check; expected set derived from source, never hardcoded.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN the mirror exists and lacks a skill present in the bundled codex source tree THEN doctor reports a warning naming the missing skill with the install remediation.
- [x] AC-2: WHEN the mirror exists and every source skill is present with matching content THEN the check reports ok.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN the workspace has no .agents/skills directory THEN the check reports ok (mirror not provisioned is not drift).
- [x] AC-4: The expected set is derived by scanning the bundled source tree so a newly added source skill is detected without code changes.
- [x] AC-5: WHEN the workspace commits a mirror-exclusions manifest (.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json) THEN the expected set SHALL be the source scan minus the manifest's excluded skills (drift detection for all other skills unchanged); WHEN the manifest is absent or unparseable the full source scan applies.
- [x] AC-6: THE speckiwi repository SHALL commit the manifest excluding kiwi-step and kiwi-wave-master (FR-FLOW-029 intentional exclusion) so the repo steady-state mirror check reports ok while the hermeticity sentinels (test/support/repo-hermeticity.ts) stay registered, and a test SHALL assert the manifest and the .agents/skills sentinel entries stay consistent.

### FR-NODE-084 — speckiwi init global skill provisioning under --global with per-agent presence gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | init-onboarding, skill-install, global |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When the global skill-provisioning option is enabled on the `initProject` service, init performs an additional skill-provisioning pass at global scope for the fixed Claude and Codex agent pair, in addition to the existing project-scope pass. The global pass is gated per agent by agent-home presence: Claude is provisioned into `~/.claude/skills` only when the Claude home directory `~/.claude` exists, and Codex is provisioned into `${CODEX_HOME:-~/.codex}/skills` only when that resolved Codex home directory exists. An agent whose home directory is absent is skipped with a warning and no global skills directory is created for it. For each present agent the global pass reuses the existing skill installer's install/update/skip/conflict classification and the `_shared` mirror sync, folding results into init's created/updated/skipped/removed/warnings report. The orphan `kiwi-*` prune runs only at project scope; at global scope no prune is performed, so skills provisioned by another project or a different speckiwi version on the shared home are left untouched. Any per-agent failure (a Result error or an unexpected filesystem error) degrades to a warning and does not abort init. The project-scope provisioning pass still runs and is unaffected. In dry-run mode a per-skill plan is produced for present agents with zero filesystem writes.

#### Rationale

init previously provisioned skills only at project scope (FR-NODE-068 AC-5), so a global agent's stale kiwi skills were never refreshed by init. The user asked init --global to also update global skills, but only for agents actually installed, to avoid creating skill directories for agents the user does not use. Agent-home directory presence is a non-invasive, cross-platform signal that the agent is in use, chosen over PATH-binary probing.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given the global option is enabled and both the Claude home (`~/.claude`) and the resolved Codex home (`${CODEX_HOME:-~/.codex}`) exist, when init runs, then the bundled kiwi skills are installed or updated at both `~/.claude/skills` and `${CODEX_HOME:-~/.codex}/skills`, in addition to the project-scope installs.
- [x] AC-2: Given the global option is enabled and the resolved Codex home does not exist, when init runs, then Codex global skills are not installed or updated, no `~/.codex/skills` directory is created, and the skip is recorded as a warning; Claude global provisioning still proceeds when `~/.claude` exists.
- [x] AC-3: Given the global option is enabled and the Claude home `~/.claude` does not exist, when init runs, then Claude global skills are not installed or updated, no `~/.claude/skills` directory is created, and the skip is recorded as a warning; Codex global provisioning still proceeds when its resolved home exists.
- [x] AC-4: Given the global option is enabled and neither agent home exists, when init runs, then no global skills directory is created for either agent, each skip is recorded as a warning, and the project-scope provisioning still completes.
- [x] AC-5: When the global option is enabled, the global pass is additive: the project-scope skill provisioning (`.claude/skills`, `.agents/skills`) still runs and its results are still reported.
- [x] AC-6: The global pass reuses the installer's update/skip/conflict classification (a managed skill with changed content is updated, a byte-identical one is skipped) but does not run the orphan `kiwi-*` prune at the global destination, so every other global skill — managed by another project or speckiwi version, user-authored, or non-`kiwi-*` — is left untouched.
- [x] AC-7: In dry-run mode with the global option enabled, present agents produce a per-skill plan and no filesystem writes occur, creating no global skills directories.

### FR-NODE-085 — Init refreshes bundled rules documents by default without the force flag

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When init runs without the force flag, it SHALL replace the installed authoring rules documents with the bundled ones and remove the stale versioned rules files it supersedes, while leaving the requirements index, the scope SRS documents and every other author-owned file untouched.

#### Rationale

Consumers currently have no safe way to receive updated rules. The non-destructive path skips the rules documents once they exist, and the only refresh path is the force flag, which the same tool recommends: doctor's remediation for a rules drift names it. That flag rewrites the requirements index and the scope SRS documents from templates, so following the tool's own advice destroys the consumer's requirements, and validation still reports zero errors afterwards because the templates are internally valid. This repository is presently in exactly that drift state. Making a plain init own the rules documents removes the need for the destructive flag and keeps the installed rules, the index pointer and the bundled constant in agreement.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Init without the force flag SHALL overwrite the installed rules documents with the bundled content whenever the two differ, and SHALL report them as refreshed rather than skipped.
- [x] AC-2: Init without the force flag SHALL remove rules files under the rules directory that match the tool's own rules-file naming pattern but carry a version other than the bundled one, so a consumer is never left holding two rules documents that disagree.
- [x] AC-3: Init SHALL NOT remove files in the rules directory that do not match the tool's rules-file naming pattern, because a consumer may keep its own documents there.
- [x] AC-4: Init without the force flag SHALL leave the requirements index, the scope SRS documents, the appendix and the step state file untouched when they already exist, apart from the single rules pointer row covered by AC-5, so refreshing rules can never destroy authored requirements.
- [x] AC-5: The refresh SHALL update the rules pointer recorded in the requirements index metadata to the bundled version when it differs, editing that row alone and leaving every other line of the index intact, so the index, the installed file and the bundled constant agree after a plain init.
- [x] AC-6: The rules filename, the index rules pointer and the bundled version SHALL all derive from a single version constant, so raising that constant cannot leave the installed filename or the index pointer behind.
- [x] AC-7: The doctor remediation for a rules version drift SHALL name a command that refreshes the rules documents without overwriting the requirements index or the scope SRS documents, and SHALL NOT name the force flag, because that flag rewrites both from templates.
- [x] AC-8: The doctor rules check SHALL verify that the rules file named by the bundled version exists on disk, so a missing rules document is reported rather than passing on the strength of an index pointer alone.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/init-rules-refresh.fr-node-085.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 10 cases, all green. Each builds a real project in a temp dir and calls initProject with no options (force unset), then reads the file system. AC-1: a locally tampered SRS rules file and a tampered SDS rules file are both restored byte-for-byte to the bundled content, and the refreshed path appears in output.updated and not in output.skipped. AC-2: SRS-MD-Rules-v0.9.0.md and SDS-MD-Rules-v0.9.0.md are both deleted while the bundled-version documents survive; output.removed is asserted for the SRS case, and the SDS case asserts the deletion on disk only. AC-3: our-house-style.md and the near-miss SRS-MD-Rules-draft.md keep their exact content, stay in readdir and are absent from output.removed. AC-4: an authored index, an authored scope SRS carrying FR-ARCH-001, an authored appendix and an authored steps/state.md are each asserted byte-identical after init, plus an explicit assertion that the requirement ID FR-ARCH-001 is still present in the scope document. AC-5: an index whose Rules row points at v0.9.0 has that row raised to the bundled filename, the stale filename is gone, and the authored Completed Work Log row plus the Target Map and Completed Work Log sections survive the surgical edit. AC-6: the installed filename and the index pointer are both asserted to equal the value derived from BUNDLED_RULES_VERSION, and a source scan asserts that bootstrap/templates.ts, bootstrap/init-project.ts and health/doctor.ts contain zero SRS-MD-Rules-v<x.y.z> filename literals. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/health/doctor-rules-drift.fr-node-085.test.ts | AC-7, AC-8 | 4 cases, all green. Each copies the valid-basic fixture workspace, manipulates the index Rules pointer and the on-disk rules document, then runs parseWorkspace plus diagnoseHealth and selects the SRS rules check. AC-7: with the index pointing at v0.9.0 the check is not ok, its remediation does not match /--force/ and does match /speckiwi init/; a second case sweeps all three reachable states of the check (drifted pointer, missing Rules row, matching pointer) and asserts no remediation in any state mentions the force flag. The named command was confirmed to exist by running `node ./bin/speckiwi init --help`, which lists init with --force as a separate opt-in flag, so the remediation names a real command and a plain invocation of it. AC-8: an index pointer matching the bundled version with the rules file deleted from disk is not reported ok, its message names the bundled-version filename, and its remediation again names `speckiwi init` without the force flag; the paired case asserts the same pointer with the document installed is reported ok, so the check discriminates on disk presence rather than on the index pointer alone. |
| VE-3 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | Independent per-AC audit, 2026-07-30, every AC mutation-checked and reverted: the byte-equality refresh gate loosened → 3 failed; the prune delete and its report suppressed → 2 and 1 failed; the rules-filename pattern widened to any .md → 1 failed; the force gate forced open → 1 failed; the Rules-row match and the surgical write broken → 1 failed each; the filename decoupled from the version constant → 2 failed; --force re-added to the doctor remediation → 2 failed; the disk-presence gate forced true → 1 failed. All 8 CONFIRMED. The audit found one overstatement in VE-1 — the SDS half of AC-2 asserts the on-disk deletion but not output.removed — which VE-1 now states accurately. |

RULES_DOCUMENT_FILENAME_PATTERN is the anchored prefix-and-version filename test that draws the AC-2 / AC-3 boundary. The SDS document has a parallel BUNDLED_SDS_RULES_VERSION and BUNDLED_SDS_RULES_FILENAME pair; it is installed and pruned by the same code path but has no index pointer. |

It stats docs/rule/<BUNDLED_SRS_RULES_FILENAME> and warns with a message naming that path when the document is absent, so a matching index pointer alone can no longer produce an ok verdict (AC-8). The shared remediation string names a plain `speckiwi init` and never the force flag, and it states that init leaves the requirements index and the scope SRS documents untouched (AC-7); the missing-Rules-row branch instead asks the author to add the row, because init refreshes a stale pointer but never inserts one into the author-owned index. The bundled path and the version string are both derived from templates.ts constants, so no versioned filename literal remains in this module (AC-6). |

### FR-NODE-086 — Bundled rules and injected agent instructions are English, with a migration for the previous Korean block

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

The bundled authoring rules documents and every part of the agent instruction block that init writes into the agent files SHALL be written in English, and init SHALL replace a previously injected Korean-headed block in place rather than leaving it and appending a second block.

#### Rationale

The installed material is mixed-language: the rules document is largely Korean while the instruction block body, the skills and every diagnostic message are English. A consumer who does not read Korean cannot use the rules document that the instruction block tells them to read. Changing the heading and the end marker is what makes this risky rather than cosmetic: init finds an existing block by matching its heading and then its end marker, so switching both to English would make every previously initialised repository unfindable and init would append a second block instead of replacing the first. The repository would then carry two managed blocks that disagree, which is the same failure the rules document's own section 40 already produces by shipping a stale block. The migration entry point is therefore part of the change, not a follow-up.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The bundled authoring rules documents that init installs SHALL contain no Hangul, so a consumer reading the installed rules never meets a language the rest of the installed material does not use.
- [x] AC-2: The translation SHALL preserve the normative content of every rule: the diagnostic code table, the identifier and heading grammars, the section numbering and the requirement-block field names SHALL survive unchanged, because those are the contract the runtime enforces.
- [x] AC-3: The agent instruction block SHALL contain no Hangul in any part, including its heading and its end marker, so the whole injected block is one language.
- [x] AC-4: The agent instruction version SHALL be raised, because the injected text changes and a consumer must be able to tell the two apart by version alone.
- [x] AC-5: Init SHALL recognise a previously injected block whose heading is the Korean form and SHALL replace it from that heading through its end marker, so a repository that was initialised before this change ends with exactly one instruction block.
- [x] AC-6: After that replacement the content a consumer wrote outside the block SHALL survive byte-identical, as it does for a same-language upgrade.
- [x] AC-7: A repository that already carries the English block SHALL be recognised by the existing versioned path, so the Korean recognition is an additional entry point rather than a replacement for it.
- [x] AC-8: The repository's own agent files SHALL carry the English block, so the tool and the repository that ships it do not disagree about what init produces.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/agent-instruction-english.fr-node-086.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | 21/21 pass; one describe per AC, every behavioural case runs initProject against a real temp repo. AC-1: after init, docs/rule holds exactly the two bundled files and both are Hangul-free (superset scan covering syllables plus conjoining and compatibility jamo). AC-2: diagnostic-code set equals the runtime registry, ID/heading grammars and {PREFIX}-{SCOPE}-{NNN} asserted verbatim, all 11 prefix-to-type rows, 10 metadata field names, 8 canonical section headings, index structure names, and 42 contiguous top-level section numbers. AC-3: heading/end-marker constants and the rendered snippet are Hangul-free and the Korean forms are absent from both written files. AC-4: version compares above the superseded 1.6 and the old heading is gone. AC-5: Korean-headed fixture migrates to exactly one English block on both LF and CRLF input, and a second init is a no-op. AC-6: text before and after the block compared verbatim, including an indented block and a pipe-bearing bullet. AC-7: an already-current file is reported skipped and left byte-identical; an older English block is replaced in place. AC-8: repository root read with on-disk casing resolution. Full suite at the 2026-07-30 audit: 222 files / 2352 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed under the repository's serial run; lint, build and validate --fail-on-warning (0/0) pass. Note that the suite is not stable under file parallelism — an audit observed 3 to 4 timeout and MCP-connection flakes with a different failing set each run, all passing in isolation and none attributable to this requirement. |

Both go through findVersionedBlock, the strict path that requires a heading and its matching end marker and rejects a pair with an intervening top-level heading — the Korean form is deliberately not wired to the marker-less LEGACY_AGENT_HEADING_PATTERN fallback, so a consumer's prose can never be swallowed. A Korean match is always replaced rather than skipped; since FR-NODE-089 the skip decision compares the block's content to the shipped snippet rather than reading a version, and a Korean block never matches it. buildVersionedHeadingPattern derives both patterns from the shipped heading constants and tolerates CRLF. AC-6: replaceAgentInstructionBlock preserves the text on both sides. |

### FR-NODE-087 — Bundled authoring rules document every syntax the tool writes

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |

| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The bundled SRS-MD authoring rules document that `speckiwi init` installs shall document every SRS-MD syntax the runtime itself writes, so a consumer following the installed rules never reverts tool-written content, and both bundled rules documents shall be shipped at the rules version the tool declares.

#### Rationale

The installed v1.0.0 rules forbid heading emphasis and never mention the `[DISCARDED]` / `[DRAFT]` markers or the `checked_compatible` relation, while `update_status`, `update_stability` and `add_compatibility_check` write exactly those. A consumer following the installed rules would revert tool-written markers by hand and break the marker-to-status coupling the mutations maintain.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The bundled SRS rules document lists `checked_compatible` among its Relation Values.
- [x] AC-2: The bundled SRS rules document specifies the `checked_compatible` Notes token grammar with the recognised keys `fpv`, `self`, `peer` and `checked-at`, and the semanticSha normalisation contract the runtime hashes.
- [x] AC-3: The bundled SRS rules document specifies the discarded heading marker — heading strikethrough plus `[DISCARDED]` — its successor slot, and its removal when the requirement leaves the discarded status.
- [x] AC-4: The bundled SRS rules document specifies the draft heading marker `[DRAFT — pending decision]`, its successor slot, that it carries no strikethrough, and its removal when the stability leaves draft.
- [x] AC-5: The bundled SRS rules document states that a mutation targets exactly one Requirement ID and states the three mutation tool kinds the schemas declare.
- [x] AC-6: The bundled SRS rules document states the non-standard heading marker policy and how the governing rules version is identified from the Rules pointer.
- [x] AC-7: `BUNDLED_RULES_VERSION` and `BUNDLED_SDS_RULES_VERSION` are `2.5.0`, and the installed rules file name, the index Rules pointer and the packaged rules path all derive from those constants.
- [x] AC-8: The package file whitelist names both bundled rules documents at the declared version, so a packaged install resolves each bundled document from disk.
- [x] AC-9: Every statement the document makes about runtime behaviour matches the runtime: no diagnostic code absent from the diagnostic registry and no enforcement the runtime does not perform appear in the document.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/bundled-rules-completeness.fr-node-087.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9 | 19 cases, all green. The content assertions read the document the tool ships and installs, not a fixture. AC-9 is enforced mechanically: every SRS-Ennn / SRS-Wnnn token in the document is matched against the diagnostic registry, which is what caught the v3.0.0 draft citing SRS-W042 and SRS-W043, codes the registry never defined. |
| VE-2 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-6, AC-9 | Independent per-AC audit, 2026-07-30. It found AC-9 false: the ported §30.4 claimed the parser reports an unknown-marker warning, which no diagnostic and no bracket sub-parser implements — confirmed empirically by validating a heading carrying [OBSOLETE] and getting no marker diagnostic. §30.4 was rewritten to state only what the tool does. The audit also found that AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 and AC-6 each passed with the very text they pin deleted, because the asserted token recurred elsewhere in the document; all four assertions are now section-scoped and each was mutation-checked to fail when its section text is altered. |

### FR-NODE-088 — Scope document numbers are allocated sequentially and never collide

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Every scope SRS document the tool creates shall receive a two-digit ordering number that is one above the highest number already present among the project's scope documents, starting at `01` in a project that has none, so no two scope documents ever share a leading number and no existing document is renumbered.

#### Rationale

`parseScopeOption` returned a hard-coded `10.<slug>.srs.md` for every scope, so every document the template path created claimed the same number 10, and `scaffoldScope` allocated decades rather than the next ordering number. Users observed scope documents numbered 10 and 20 where 01 and 02 were expected, and whole document sets where every file carried the number 10.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A scope document created in a project that holds no scope SRS document is named `01.<slug>.srs.md`, unless a non-scope document under `docs/spec` already holds that number, in which case allocation advances to the lowest free one — see `FR-NODE-097`, which supersedes the unconditional form of this criterion.
- [x] AC-2: A scope document created in a project whose highest existing scope document number is N is named with N+1, so a project numbered 10 through 60 receives 61 and a project numbered 01 receives 02.
- [x] AC-3: The allocated number is rendered as two digits, so a number below ten carries a leading zero.
- [x] AC-4: The scope template helper carries no fixed document number of its own — the number of every document the tool writes comes from allocation against the documents already on disk.
- [x] AC-5: `speckiwi init` scaffolds its default scope document only when the project holds no scope SRS document, so an init in a project that already has scope documents adds none.
- [x] AC-6: An allocated number is never one that an existing scope document already uses, and no existing document is renamed or renumbered by the allocation.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/scope-document-numbering.fr-node-088.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 16 cases, all green. Each case builds a real project on disk and reads the resulting file names. Red run before the fix: 12 of the original 13 failed, the survivor being the AC-6 non-regression guard the old allocator also satisfied; three cases were added afterwards for the AC-5 guard, the identity of a pre-existing document, and an explicit --scope that init does not act on. |
| VE-2 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | Independent per-AC audit, 2026-07-30. Each AC was mutation-checked: the implementation was broken in the smallest way that violates that AC and the cited test confirmed to fail, then reverted. AC-1 first document forced to 10 → 1 failed; AC-2 next-decade allocator → 3 failed; AC-3 padStart dropped → 1 failed; AC-4 a document field re-added to the parse result → 1 failed on each return path; AC-5 the no-scope-document gate removed → 4 failed; AC-6 lowest-free-gap allocator → 1 failed. All six CONFIRMED. |
| VE-3 | test | test/core/bootstrap/init-scope-number-collision.fr-node-097.test.ts | AC-1, AC-4 | 6 cases, all green. Covers the amended AC-1 exception: a project holding its own 01 document receives 02, a run of occupied numbers advances to the lowest free one, and a project with no numbered document of its own still receives 01. Red before the fix: 4 of 5 failed. The AC-4 case greps every scopeDocumentName call site for a literal number and caught a second hardcoded site in renderIndexTemplate that the original audit missed. |

### FR-NODE-089 — Init repairs a drifted agent instruction block

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

`speckiwi init` shall replace the managed agent instruction block whenever its content differs from the snippet the tool ships, not only when the block's version differs, so a block that drifted from the shipped text — or that names a path the tool no longer ships — is repaired rather than kept forever.

#### Rationale

`upsertAgentInstruction` skipped whenever the block version matched the shipped constant, so any hand edit inside the block survived every future init. The repository's own CLAUDE.md had drifted by one sentence, and raising the bundled rules version to 2.5.0 made the defect load-bearing: every existing consumer's block would keep citing an SDS rules path the tool no longer ships.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Init replaces a managed block whose body differs from the shipped snippet even though the block heading carries the current version, and reports the file as updated.
- [x] AC-2: Init leaves a managed block that already matches the shipped snippet untouched and reports the file as skipped, so a repeated init writes nothing.
- [x] AC-3: The shipped snippet names the Completed Work Log history file, so the sentence the repository's CLAUDE.md carried by hand is part of the block the tool writes.
- [x] AC-4: The shipped snippet cites the installed SDS authoring rules path derived from the bundled SDS rules constant, so a rules version bump cannot leave the block naming a document the tool no longer ships.
- [x] AC-5: After an init, the managed block in each of AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md is identical to the shipped snippet, and content outside the block is preserved.

#### Verification Evidence

Red run before the fix: the three drift cases failed while the skip/idempotence guards passed, so the change is confined to the repair path. A CRLF case was added afterwards, because comparing line endings would have rewritten every Windows checkout on every init. |
| VE-2 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | Independent per-AC audit, 2026-07-30, every AC mutation-checked and reverted. AC-1 skip condition reverted to version-only → 2 failed; AC-2 never-skip → 2 failed and normalizeEol made the identity → the CRLF case failed; AC-3 the history-file clause removed from the snippet → 1 failed; AC-4 the SDS path hard-coded → 1 failed; AC-5 block replacement made to drop surrounding content → 1 failed. All five CONFIRMED. The audit also noted that VE-1's "three drift cases failed" is attributable to two changes in one commit rather than to the skip-condition fix alone. |

### FR-NODE-090 — Shared resource prune keeps files a mirror-excluded skill references

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The shared Kiwi resource prune that follows a skill install shall treat a shared file referenced by a skill named in the destination's mirror-exclusions manifest as referenced, so installing one skill never deletes a shared contract that an intentionally unmirrored skill depends on.

#### Rationale

`speckiwi skills install codex <skill>` deleted `.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md` on every run, reproduced five times. The prune collects references by walking the destination tree only, and the sole skill referencing that contract, `kiwi-wave-master`, is named in this repository's mirror-exclusions manifest, so it is never in the destination and its references never enter the retained set. A shared contract referenced by an excluded skill is still referenced.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A shared resource referenced only by a skill named in the destination's mirror-exclusions manifest survives an install of a different skill.
- [x] AC-2: A shared resource referenced by no skill at all — neither installed nor excluded — is still pruned, so the prune keeps doing the job it exists for.
- [x] AC-3: A destination with no exclusions manifest, or with an unparseable one, prunes exactly as before, so the repair adds no behaviour to workspaces that never excluded anything.
- [x] AC-4: A skill named in the manifest but absent from the source tree contributes no references and causes no failure.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/skills/shared-prune-exclusions.fr-node-090.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 6 cases, all green. Red run before the fix: only the two retention cases failed, so the prune's existing behaviour is unchanged. Confirmed against this repository as well — `speckiwi skills install codex kiwi-pm` now leaves .agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md in place, where it previously deleted it on every run. |
| VE-2 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | Independent per-AC audit, 2026-07-30, every AC mutation-checked and reverted. AC-1 exclusions loop source emptied → 2 failed; AC-2 the prune removal deleted → 1 failed; AC-3 absent and unparseable manifests made to return the excluded name → 1 failed each; AC-4 the SKILL.md presence guard removed → 1 failed. All four CONFIRMED. The premise was corroborated against real data: the manifest lists kiwi-step and kiwi-wave-master, the wave-master skill is the only referrer of waves-event.md, and installing kiwi-pm now leaves that contract in place. |

### FR-NODE-091 — Project upgrade repairs the migration gaps init leaves to the author

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | upgrade, migration, init |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/next/2026-07-31.speckiwi-upgrade-command.md |

#### Requirement

The project upgrade operation shall bring an existing project current in one call: it delegates the tool-owned refresh to the init operation, inserts a `| Rules |` row into the index metadata table when that table has none, and rewrites a reference to a rules document that the refresh does not leave installed — in both the path form (`SRS-MD-Rules-v<old>.md`) and the prose form (`SRS-MD Authoring Rules v<old>`) — in the agent instruction files it manages. A dangling reference found elsewhere under `docs/`, outside `docs/spec/` and `docs/rule/`, is reported and never rewritten, because a note that names a retired rules version is a record rather than a defect. The operation defaults to a plan-only run that writes nothing while still reporting every change it would make, each with its file path and line, and performs the plan only when the caller asks. It resolves the project by discovery rather than assuming the working directory, never renumbers a scope document, never overwrites an existing hook, and never writes a requirement body; both of its Rules-row edits are confined to the `| Field | Value |` metadata table, so an author's row of the same shape elsewhere in the index is never overwritten. Under `docs/spec/` it writes that Rules row and, through the refresh, the tool-owned scaffolds init creates when they are missing.

#### Rationale

Running the v2.5.0 init against a simulated old-version consumer project upgraded most things and left two measured gaps. A reference to a pruned rules document is not repaired: this repository's own CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md carried exactly that dangling line and had to be fixed by hand, as did three shipped skills. A missing index Rules row is never inserted, because `refreshIndexRulesPointer` only ever replaces an existing row, so `doctor` warns forever and its own remediation admits it. Both repairs edit author-owned content, which init deliberately does not do — that boundary is what closed the `--force` data-loss defect — so they belong to an explicit opt-in migration that defaults to dry-run.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With no apply request the operation writes nothing — the workspace tree is byte-identical after the call — and its result still reports every change it would have made, both the repairs and the report-only findings.
- [x] AC-2: An index metadata table carrying no `| Rules |` row gains exactly one, holding the bundled rules pointer, with every other metadata row and the authored index body unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: An index that already carries a `| Rules |` row gains no second one; refreshing that row stays init's surgical edit.
- [x] AC-4: A path-form reference to an absent rules document in a managed agent instruction file is rewritten to the bundled filename, and the change is reported with its file path and line number.
- [x] AC-5: A prose-form citation of an absent rules document is rewritten by the same pass, so matching only the path form does not satisfy this requirement.
- [x] AC-6: A reference is judged against the rules documents the refresh leaves installed rather than the ones present before it, and the author-owned repairs run before the refresh, so a plan reports exactly the repairs an apply performs — including for a project that already carries a managed agent block, which is the normal case for the projects this operation targets.
- [x] AC-7: A dangling reference outside the managed agent files is reported and never rewritten. The reported set is the markdown under `docs/` excluding two directories the tool owns or governs: `docs/spec/`, which is never scanned at all, and `docs/rule/`, which the refresh itself installs and prunes. No requirement body is written. Under `docs/spec/` the operation writes the index metadata Rules row and, through the refresh, the tool-owned scaffolds init creates when they are missing — `90.appendix.md` and `steps/state.md`.
- [x] AC-8: A pre-existing git hook is left byte-identical and reported as `pre-existing`, while a hook the refresh itself creates is not, so the report distinguishes what was left alone from what was installed.
- [x] AC-9: The tool-owned refresh is the init operation's own: after an apply against a project holding stale rules documents, `docs/rule` contains exactly the bundled documents and the stale ones are reported as removed.
- [x] AC-10: Both Rules-row edits — the insertion and init's refresh of an existing row — are confined to the `| Field | Value |` metadata table, matched by position as well as by shape. A scope named `Rules` keeps its SRS Documents and Scope Map rows, an author's two-cell `| Rules | … |` row elsewhere in the document is neither overwritten nor allowed to suppress the insertion, an index opening with another table still gets the row in the metadata table, and an existing row written without the template's spacing is refreshed rather than duplicated.
- [x] AC-11: A repair preserves the file's line endings byte for byte — on the insertion path, on init's refresh path, and for a file whose endings are already mixed — and an index whose metadata table ends at EOF with no trailing newline gains a well-formed row without welding it onto the previous one or acquiring a trailing newline.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/upgrade-project.fr-node-091.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9, AC-10, AC-11 | 20 cases, all green. The first 9 were written before the implementation and confirmed red (the module did not exist); the later ones were added from audit findings, each confirmed red by reverting its fix — disabling the metadata-range guard, moving the insertion terminator after the row, and dropping CR preservation failed exactly their own three cases and no others. Independent per-AC mutation audit 2026-07-30 applied 23 probes and found every AC mutation-sensitive. |
| VE-2 | review | docs/next/2026-07-31.speckiwi-upgrade-command.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-4, AC-5, AC-7, AC-9 | End-to-end run of the shipped CLI against a simulated old-version project (v1.0.0 SRS+SDS rules on disk, both agent files citing them in both spellings, an index with no Rules row, a docs/guide note, a requirement body citing the old version). Plan: workspace byte-identical, 6 findings reported. Apply: both agent files rewritten in both spellings, one Rules row inserted at line 7, both stale rules documents pruned, docs/guide/history.md byte-identical and reported as `report`, docs/spec never reported. |

### FR-NODE-092 — Guarded repair for a dangling rules reference in requirement metadata

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | repair, migration, related-docs |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md |

#### Requirement

A guarded repair operation shall find every requirement whose `Related Docs` metadata row references a rules document that is not installed under `docs/rule`, and rewrite that reference to the bundled filename. It diagnoses without writing and performs the repair only when asked. It rewrites nothing but the reference text inside that one metadata row, records a Change Note on each repaired requirement, and is permitted whatever the requirement's Status — the row is metadata rather than a body field, and `add_related_doc` already writes it at any status. A reference whose document is installed is left alone.

#### Rationale

Five verified requirements — `FR-PARSE-013`, `FR-PARSE-016`, `FR-NODE-012`, `FR-NODE-015`, `FR-FLOW-009` — have carried a broken `Related Docs` link to `../rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md` since the v2.5.0 rules rename, and `speckiwi links check` reports all five. No path repaired them: `edit_requirement_fields` refuses any granular edit on a verified requirement, and `speckiwi upgrade` excludes `docs/spec/` by design because a migration rewriting requirement bodies is exactly what the tool-owned/author-owned split prevents. Demoting five verified requirements to correct a file path is the wrong trade. A path correction that changes no meaning is a repair, not an edit, so it belongs beside the requirement-id collision repair rather than inside either of those two.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Diagnose lists every requirement whose `Related Docs` row references a rules document absent from `docs/rule`, naming the requirement id, the file, the line and the reference.
- [x] AC-2: Diagnose writes nothing: the workspace tree is byte-identical afterwards.
- [x] AC-3: An apply rewrites only the reference text inside the `Related Docs` row — the row's other references, its formatting and its link text survive.
- [x] AC-4: An apply succeeds on a `verified` requirement and records a Change Note on it, so a metadata correction is auditable rather than silent.
- [x] AC-5: No other part of a repaired requirement changes: the statement, acceptance criteria, verification evidence and trace links are byte-identical afterwards.
- [x] AC-6: A `Related Docs` reference whose rules document is installed is left unchanged.
- [x] AC-7: The repair is opt-in: without an apply request nothing is written, and the diagnosis reports exactly what an apply would perform.
- [x] AC-8: After an apply against this repository, `speckiwi links check` reports zero broken rules-document references.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Independent per-AC audit 2026-07-30 ran 11 mutation probes; every AC failed its cited test when broken. The audit found the 'every requirement' plurality untested (findings.slice(0,1) left all cases green), so four two-requirement cases were added and re-probed red - that is the shape the real repository case had, five requirements sharing one stale reference. |

### FR-NODE-093 — A table-row edit rejects an unknown column key instead of reporting a write that changed nothing

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mutation, input-validation, honesty |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md |

#### Requirement

The requirement table-row edit shall reject any `values` key that is not a settable column of the target section, failing with `USAGE`, naming the offending key and the accepted keys, and writing nothing. It shall not silently ignore an unrecognised key, because the row is rebuilt from the existing cells and an ignored key produces a byte-identical replacement reported as a successful write.

#### Rationale

Passing `values: { Covers: …, Notes: … }` — the capitalisation the rendered table header uses — returned `ok: true`, `written: true` and a `replaceLine` operation whose replacement equalled the original. The evidence correction it claimed to make never happened, and only re-reading the file revealed it. A caller that trusts the envelope records a governance edit that does not exist. Found on 2026-07-30 by the per-AC audit of `IR-CLI-076`–`078`, then hit immediately afterwards while correcting an evidence row.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: An unknown `values` key fails with `USAGE`, names the offending key, and writes nothing.
- [x] AC-2: The failure message names the accepted keys for the target section, so the caller can correct the call without reading the source.
- [x] AC-3: A capitalised variant of a real column — `Covers` for `covers` — is rejected rather than ignored, and no successful-write envelope is returned for it.
- [x] AC-4: Every settable column still updates its cell: `type`, `reference`, `covers` and `notes` for a verification evidence row; `type`, `reference`, `relation` and `notes` for a trace link row.
- [x] AC-5: The derived evidence row id is not settable, so passing `id` is rejected rather than silently dropped.

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-NODE-094 — A requirement created at draft stability carries the draft heading marker

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | mutation, stability, marker |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md |

#### Requirement

A requirement rendered with `Stability=draft` shall carry the `[DRAFT — pending decision]` heading marker, whether it reached draft by creation or by transition, so the marker is a reliable signal of draft stability rather than a signal of how the requirement got there. The bundled authoring rules shall state the creation default so an author is not surprised by a stability they did not choose.

#### Rationale

`add_requirement` defaults stability to `draft` and renders a bare heading, while `update_stability` writing the same value renders the marker. The marker therefore encoded provenance rather than state, which defeats the purpose §30.2 gives it: a reader cannot use it to spot a draft requirement, and a project's drafts look like two different kinds of thing. §37's minimal template compounds the confusion by showing `evolving`, so nothing in the document told an author what the tool would actually write.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A requirement created with `Stability=draft` renders the `[DRAFT — pending decision]` marker on its heading.
- [x] AC-2: A requirement created with any other stability renders a bare heading, unchanged from before.
- [x] AC-3: A requirement created without an explicit stability gets the documented default and the marker that matches it, so the default and the marker never disagree.
- [x] AC-4: The rendered heading round-trips: parsing it yields the same id and title, so the marker does not corrupt the id the rest of the tool keys on.
- [x] AC-5: The bundled authoring rules state which stability `add_requirement` applies when the caller omits it.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/draft-creation-marker.fr-node-094.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | Audit 2026-07-30: probes on the marker spread, an always-on marker, and a malformed marker position each failed. The auditor additionally ran add_requirement end to end and read the written heading, so AC-1 holds through the real mutation and not only the renderer. AC-5 verified by inspection: exactly one line in the bundled document matches both add_requirement and omit. |

### FR-NODE-095 — the diagnostic registry lists exactly the codes that can be emitted

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | diagnostics, registry, rules |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The diagnostic registry and the bundled rules document's validation table SHALL list exactly the codes the validators can emit. Eleven codes that no path can honour SHALL be retracted from both. Two codes in the `SRS-` namespace that step validation does emit, `SRS-W044` and `SRS-W045`, SHALL be registered and tabled, because a user who sees a code in output must be able to look it up and today `explain` reports it unknown. The table section SHALL state that it lists every code `validate-spec` can emit and only those, and SHALL name the two surfaces that report findings outside it: release readiness, which returns typed result fields, and step validation, whose remaining advisories live in the `SDS-` and `STEP_` namespaces the `SRS-` contract does not reach. Where a retracted code corresponded to a section the document presents as a rule, that section SHALL state that a reviewer applies it and that no diagnostic reports a match. The document SHALL NOT name a retracted code anywhere, so the existing contract that it cites no unregistered code keeps holding without an exemption. The per-code reason SHALL be recorded on this requirement instead. A retracted code SHALL NOT be reused for a different meaning.

#### Rationale

A code that is registered and advertised but unemittable is a claim the tool does not honour, which is worse than an absent feature: it makes the validation table read as enforced. For the release-readiness codes the finding is not missing, only the surface is wrong. For the wording and tag codes the evidence is measured rather than assumed — a word-list matcher for the discouraged expressions fires on eighteen occurrences in this repository's own specification and every one is ordinary English, and the tag ceiling is stated as a recommendation whose enforcement would fail this repository's own warning gate with no sanctioned repair for a verified requirement's tags.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The diagnostic registry defines none of the eleven retracted codes, and no path in the source emits any of them.
- [x] AC-2: The rules document's validation table lists none of the eleven, and the document does not name any of them anywhere else either, so the existing contract that it cites no unregistered code keeps holding rather than needing an exemption.
- [x] AC-3: `SRS-W044` and `SRS-W045` are registered with the severity, title and message the step validator actually emits, are listed in the table, and `explain` resolves each instead of reporting an unknown code.
- [x] AC-4: The validation table section states that it lists every code `validate-spec` can emit and only those, and names both surfaces that report outside it: release readiness with its typed result fields, and step validation with its `SDS-` and `STEP_` advisories.
- [x] AC-5: The discouraged-expression list states that a reviewer applies it and that no diagnostic reports a match, and the tag ceiling states that it is a recommendation validation does not report.
- [x] AC-6: The per-code reason for each retraction is recorded on this requirement rather than in the shipped document, because naming a retracted code there would collide with the contract that the document cites no unregistered code.
- [x] AC-7: The registry and the validation table agree in both directions, and every registry code this requirement does not name is still present with its severity and title unchanged.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/diagnostic-retraction.fr-node-095.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 13 cases, all green. Audit 2026-07-30 confirmed no path emits any retracted code and that the bidirectional registry-table pin holds, but found the exclusivity claim false: step validation emits SRS-W044, SRS-W045, STEP_DIRECT_CONFLICT, SDS-W050 to W053 and STEP_PROMOTE_NO_EVIDENCE outside the registry. The two SRS-namespace codes are now registered and tabled - explain resolved neither before - and the section names the rest. |

### FR-NODE-096 — six rules-document statements are corrected to the runtime they describe

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | rules, documentation, fidelity |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Six statements in the bundled rules document that describe behaviour the runtime does not have SHALL be corrected to describe what the runtime does. The status transition table and the `implemented` conditions SHALL be marked as an authoring convention the tool does not gate, naming the status gates the tool does enforce. The governing-rules-version section SHALL state that the tooling reads the `Rules` row version only to keep the row aligned with the installed document and that no runtime behaviour switches on it, because a release bundles exactly one rules version. The atomicity guarantee SHALL be scoped to the three changes inside the requirement's own file and SHALL name the index rollup as a separate follow-up write outside that transaction. The index Required Structure SHALL include the `Rules` row and name the command that writes it for a new project, the command that inserts it into an index that lacks it, and the check that reports its absence. The mutation-kind classification SHALL record that the compatibility-check tools act on a requirement pair and take `aReqId` and `bReqId` rather than `id`. The Completed Work Log section SHALL document that a legacy `docs/spec/05.completed-work.md` takes precedence over the history file when it exists.

#### Rationale

A shipped rules document is a contract that agents and authors follow literally, so a statement it makes that the runtime does not honour is a defect in its own right rather than a documentation nicety. Each of these six was found by a fidelity pass and left open because it was a pre-existing runtime gap; correcting the statement is the honest resolution where implementing the described gate would be a behaviour change with a blast radius the target does not cover.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The status transition table and the `implemented` conditions are stated as an authoring convention that no tool call enforces, and the section names the status gates the tool does enforce, so a reader cannot conclude that a disallowed transition is refused.
- [x] AC-2: The governing-rules-version section states that the version token is read to keep the `Rules` row aligned with the installed document and that no runtime behaviour selects rules by it.
- [x] AC-3: The tool-compliance section scopes the atomic write to the three changes in the requirement's own file and names the index rollup as a separate follow-up write that is not part of that transaction.
- [x] AC-4: The index Required Structure template contains the `Rules` metadata row, and the section names `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi upgrade` and `speckiwi doctor` for writing, inserting and reporting it.
- [x] AC-5: The mutation-kind classification records that the three compatibility-check tools take a requirement pair as `aReqId` and `bReqId` rather than a single `id`, so the `req-scoped` kind's `id` rule is not read as covering them.
- [x] AC-6: The Completed Work Log section documents that a legacy `docs/spec/05.completed-work.md` receives the appended row in preference to the history file when that file exists.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/rules-document-fidelity.fr-node-096.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 7 cases. Every sentence was mutation-checked by deleting it and confirming its assertion fails, and the document was restored byte-identically. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/mutation/status-transition-gates.fr-node-096.test.ts | AC-1 | 7 cases pinning AC-1 against the runtime rather than the document's own words. Added because the audit found the first correction of 14.2 was itself false - it claimed update_status writes whatever status it is given, and the runtime refuses planned to verified with MUTATION_DENIED. A document-string assertion cannot catch that class. |
| VE-3 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | Independent audit 2026-07-30 verified each corrected statement against the code: the 30.5 version gate is genuinely dead code with no production caller, the requirement-file write is a real temp-file-and-rename while the index rollup is a separate second write, all three commands do what 7.1 now claims, the compatibility tools take aReqId and bReqId, and the legacy 05.completed-work.md really takes precedence. One CRITICAL and three MEDIUM findings on 14.2 and 14.3 were fixed and re-checked. |

### FR-NODE-097 — scope allocation skips a number another document already holds

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | numbering, scaffold |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Scope document allocation SHALL skip a candidate number that any Markdown document directly under `docs/spec` already holds, so a newly scaffolded scope document never lands on a number another document occupies. The candidate SHALL remain one above the highest number among the project's scope SRS documents; only a candidate that collides SHALL advance, and it SHALL advance to the lowest number above it that no document holds. A sidecar SHALL NOT raise the starting candidate, so a project whose only scope document is `01` still receives `02`.

#### Rationale

Allocation and the collision diagnostic have to agree, otherwise the tool's own scaffold command produces a document that its own validation immediately warns about. A project whose highest scope document is `04` beside the legacy `05.completed-work.md` is exactly that case. Skipping only on collision preserves the recorded decision that sidecars do not drive allocation: counting `90.appendix.md` as the highest would push a project's second scope document to `91`, which is the outcome that decision exists to prevent.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A project whose highest scope SRS document is `04` and which also holds `docs/spec/05.completed-work.md` receives `06` for its next scope document, and the created file is at that number.
- [x] AC-2: A project whose scope documents leave the candidate number free receives that candidate unchanged, so allocation is not shifted by the presence of unrelated sidecars.
- [x] AC-3: A project whose only scope document is `01` receives `02` even though `90.appendix.md` and `91.completed-work-log.md` are present, so a sidecar does not raise the starting candidate.
- [x] AC-4: When the candidate and several numbers above it are all occupied, allocation advances to the lowest free number rather than to one above the highest occupied number.
- [x] AC-5: A document scaffolded through allocation produces no `SRS-W070` and no `SRS-W072` finding, so the scaffold and the collision diagnostics agree.
- [x] AC-6: `speckiwi init` allocates its default scope document through the same rule, so a project that carries its own `01` document receives `02` and the index registers it under the number it received; a project with no numbered document of its own still receives `01`.
- [x] AC-7: Every caller that names a scope document goes through the allocator, so no call site carries a fixed number of its own.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/scope-allocation-collision-skip.fr-node-097.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 6 cases, all green. Audit 2026-07-30 probed each AC - deleting the skip loop, inverting it, letting a sidecar raise the candidate, dropping the second argument at the call site - and every probe failed the cited test. AC-3 was additionally confirmed end to end through the CLI. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/bootstrap/init-scope-number-collision.fr-node-097.test.ts | AC-6, AC-7 | 6 cases, all green. Added for the HIGH the audit found: init hardcoded the number 1 and was the one caller that never went through the allocator, so a project holding its own 01 document got a colliding 01 scope document that the validator immediately reported as SRS-W072. Red before the fix: 4 of 5 failed. The AC-7 case greps every scopeDocumentName call site for a literal number and caught a second hardcoded site in renderIndexTemplate the audit had not reached. |
